## 5. 🔄 EVERYDAY WORKFLOWS

### You Just Ask

You never need to remember a command. Describe the task in plain words and the session handles the rest. On every prompt, the [skill advisor](#skill-advisor) reads the request and tells the agent which skill fits. When a workflow fits too, the brief names its command and how to run it: start it now, start it with its setup questions or offer it to you first.

The session automation covers the rest of the path. The [spec gate](#spec-gate) asks once which spec folder the work belongs to. The [session lifecycle](#session-lifecycle) hooks prime the context at start and save continuity at stop and compaction. Near the context limit, [session handover](#session-handover) carries the work into a fresh session.

The commands below are what runs underneath. Type one yourself only when you want a specific workflow or mode.

### Spec Kit Commands

`/speckit:complete` runs the whole path: intake, research, plan, build, verify and save. `/speckit:plan` stops after the plan, so someone can review it before any code is written. `/speckit:implement` builds from an existing plan. Each one accepts `:auto` to run without stopping or `:confirm` to pause at each step.

`/speckit:save` and `/speckit:resume` carry the work across sessions, as [Continuity](#continuity) describes. `/speckit:search` runs the trigger index and the ripgrep recipes over spec docs and skill docs. `/speckit:handoff` hands the work to a fresh session now, instead of waiting for the context limit.

### Deep Research And Deep Review

The deep loops run unattended and stop only when their own evidence says the work is done. `/deep:research` investigates a question one focused pass at a time. Results grow in one research document inside the spec folder. `/deep:review` audits code in passes, ranks every finding P0, P1 or P2 and never edits the code it reviews.

Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open below its minimum iteration count, while a P0 is active or while a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.

Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A single-executor review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults; a fan-out review lineage always reviews the four.

A loop can also spread across several AI CLIs at once. Each executor runs its own lineage, and for a review the strictest verdict wins the merge. A pool caps how many lineages run at once. A stall watchdog stops a lineage that stops making progress, and a cost cap bounds what each one spends. The [Deep Loop README](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md) describes both modes and the shared runtime.

### CLI Orchestration

Your main CLI does not have to handle every task itself. The CLI orchestration hub hands a task to another AI CLI on the same machine and reads the result back. The agent you are talking to stays in charge. It writes the brief, waits for the answer and checks it before using it.

This lets you mix subscriptions. You can work in one CLI and send research, reviews or bulk work to another, billed to that CLI's own plan. The bridges are provider agnostic. Each CLI runs whatever providers and models you have set up in it. That is why Skilled supports eight runtimes: whichever plans you already pay for, one runtime is your main CLI and the others receive the work you hand off.

You ask for it in plain words, for example "use cli-opencode to review this diff". The advisor routes the request to the matching bridge. The bridge checks that its CLI is installed and that the dispatch passes its hard rules, then runs it. The deep loops use the same bridges, so one research or review run can spread its passes across several models. [Runtimes And CLI Bridges](#10--runtimes-and-cli-bridges) lists all eight.

### Create And Update Skills, Agents And Commands

Extending Skilled follows the same framework that built it. The [sk-doc](.skilled/skills/sk-doc/README.md) hub holds a template, a set of standards and a validator for each kind of component. Ask for a new skill, agent or command in plain words and the advisor loads the matching sk-doc mode, so the result matches the rest of the repository.

Each kind also has a `/create` command for when you want the workflow directly. `/create:sk-skill` creates or updates a skill, and `/create:skill-parent` scaffolds a parent hub with its modes. `/create:agent` and `/create:command` cover agents and slash commands. `/create:readme`, `/create:changelog`, `/create:goal` and `/create:repo-rule` cover the documents around them. Every one runs with `:auto` or with `:confirm` to pause at each step.

Updating works the same way as creating. Name the component and the change, and the workflow edits it in place, checks it against its template and runs the sk-doc validator. A new skill needs no registration step, because the advisor finds it from its own metadata.

&nbsp;

## 6. 🧰 SKILLS

Fourteen skills ship with Skilled. Gate 2 loads one when the advisor matches it to a task. Seven of them are parent hubs. A hub holds several modes and routes each request to the mode that fits, 42 modes in all. The other seven skills work on their own.

### Parent Hubs

**[sk-code](.skilled/skills/sk-code/README.md)** writes, reviews and verifies code in the stack you are in. Its five modes are quality, for the checks after implementation, and review, for findings-first code review. CI covers GitHub Actions workflows and frontend covers CSS, HTML and browser work. The skilled mode holds the standards for this repository's own TypeScript, Python, shell and config code.

**[sk-doc](.skilled/skills/sk-doc/README.md)** authors documentation and components. Its fifteen modes create skills, parent hubs, READMEs, agents, commands, feature catalogs, manual testing playbooks, benchmarks, changelogs, diffs, frontmatter, repo rules and goals. Two more run quality control and apply the human voice rules.

**[sk-design](.skilled/skills/sk-design/SKILL.md)** turns one design identity into an artifact. Its three modes extract a measured style reference from a live site, draw a diagram and build a chart.

**[system-deep-loop](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md)** runs the autonomous loops over one shared runtime. Its two modes are deep research and deep review.

**[cli-external-orchestration](.skilled/skills/cli-external-orchestration/README.md)** hands work to another AI CLI. Its eight modes are bridges to OpenCode, Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Devin, Pi, DeepSeek Harness and Mistral Vibe.

**[cli-classifier](.skilled/skills/cli-classifier/README.md)** asks a hosted classifier for typed judgments. It has one mode today, the Jev transport, and future classifiers join as new modes.

**[mcp-tooling](.skilled/skills/mcp-tooling/README.md)** bridges outside tools through Code Mode. Its eight modes cover Chrome DevTools, ClickUp, the Aside browser, Notion and Obsidian. Three more are read-only design sources: Figma, Refero and Mobbin.

### Standalone Skills

**[system-spec-kit](.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/README.md)** owns spec folders, documentation levels, the validator, continuity and lexical search. Every file change goes through it.

## Phase Context

This is **Phase 14** of the expansive-depth program, and it runs last.

**Scope Boundary**: Documentation only. Operator decision 2026-11-17: each implementation phase writes its own manual testing playbook scenarios through `/create:manual-testing-playbook`. This phase verifies that coverage, updates the feature catalogs, runs the two alignment checks, fixes the doc findings and reruns. It changes no runtime script, command workflow or agent behavior.

**Dependencies**:
- All of `../001-completed-run-extension/` through `../013-no-redispatch-landed-iteration/` complete, so every documented behavior exists in code and every phase has written its scenarios.

**Deliverables**:
- A coverage check showing every row of the Feature to Playbook Map has a scenario its phase wrote.
- Updated feature catalog entries.
- A clean `/local-doc-alignment` report and a clean `/local-skilled-alignment` report.

## 2. PROBLEM & PURPOSE
<!-- FILL: State the problem in two sentences: what breaks, and for whom. GOOD: The rotate-logs script never deletes old archives, so a host that copies `logs/archive` fills its disk. WEAK: The script has issues with output. -->

### Problem Statement
Phases 001-013 add `--extend=N`, `--depth-mode=expansive` for deep-research and deep-review, custom review dimensions and defect fixes that change what an operator sees, but no system-deep-loop manual testing playbook or feature catalog mentions any of them. An operator cannot find a scenario that exercises the new flags, and a reviewer cannot check the catalogs against the shipped code.

### Purpose
Confirm that every user-visible feature and fix of 001-013 has the playbook scenario its phase wrote, give each a catalog entry, and close the program with both alignment checks reporting no open finding.
<!-- /ANCHOR:problem -->

## 3. SCOPE
<!-- FILL: List three to five things this change does, each starting with a verb. Before you list a new file, module, option, layer or dependency, name what fails today without it, or drop it. GOOD: Add a `--keep <n>` option that deletes all but the newest n archives in `logs/archive/`. WEAK: Update the script and docs. -->

### In Scope
- Verify playbook coverage: each row of the Feature to Playbook Map below has a scenario in its category, written by its owning phase through `/create:manual-testing-playbook`. A missing scenario is a finding for the owning phase, which adds it through the same command; this phase does not write scenarios.
- Update the system-deep-loop feature catalogs through `/create:feature-catalog system-deep-loop update --path <catalog dir>`.
- Run `/local-doc-alignment` on the expansive-depth topic and `/local-skilled-alignment` on the 001-013 change.
- Fix each doc finding: a playbook gap through its owning phase, a catalog finding through `/create:feature-catalog`, and a finding in a reference doc, `README.md`, `SKILL.md` or command doc by a direct edit here. Regenerate a compiled contract when a fix changes a document it digests. Rerun both checks until neither reports an open finding.

### Out of Scope
- Writing playbook scenarios. Each implementation phase owns its own (operator decision 2026-11-17).
- Runtime, command workflow or agent behavior changes. A finding that shows a behavior bug is a code finding: it is recorded in `goal.md` §4 and handed to the owning phase, which reopens with a fix task. This phase cannot close while a code finding is open, unless the operator records a waiver for it in `goal.md` §4.
- Changelog entries and release notes. Each implementation phase owns its own; `../scratch/cross-phase-handoff.md` lists which phases add one.

<!-- ANCHOR:risks -->
## 6. RISKS & DEPENDENCIES
<!-- FILL: Name one dependency and one risk. Give each a concrete mitigation. GOOD: A script that reads `--keep` with no value could delete every archive. Mitigation: reject a missing value with exit 2 and test it. WEAK: There may be some risk. -->

| Type | Item | Impact | Mitigation |
|------|------|--------|------------|
| Dependency | Phases 001-013 complete, each with its own playbook scenarios | The coverage check would fail on work not yet done | Start only when every 001-013 goal has all criteria checked |
| Risk | A phase closed without its playbook scenario | Medium | The coverage check names the gap and the owning phase reopens to add it through `/create:manual-testing-playbook` |
| Risk | An alignment finding points at code, not docs | Medium | Record it in `goal.md` §4 and hand it to the owning phase, which reopens with a fix task; do not change code here |
| Risk | The alignment loop does not converge | Low | Rerun at most three times; on a third run with open findings, record them and escalate to the operator |
<!-- /ANCHOR:risks -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:questions -->
## 7. OPEN QUESTIONS
<!-- FILL: Write each open question as one line. Write `None` when nothing blocks. GOOD: Does a missing `--keep` value delete every archive? Decision: no, a missing value exits 2. WEAK: Edge cases are unclear. -->

- Which topic string does `/local-doc-alignment` take for this program? Proposed: `expansive depth`, with `--extend` and `depth-mode` as terms if the command's topic config allows extra terms.
- Does phase 006 or 007 count as user-visible enough for its own scenario, or does a line in an existing scenario suffice? The owning phase decides when it runs the playbook command, and this phase accepts either.
<!-- /ANCHOR:questions -->

# Goal: Playbook coverage, feature catalogs and alignment for the expansive-depth program

<!-- ANCHOR:directive -->
## 1. DURABLE DIRECTIVE
<!-- FILL: Write one sentence: what the packet is for, not how. GOOD: Keep only the newest n archives on request. WEAK: Improve the script. -->

**Objective:** Confirm that every user-visible feature and fix of phases 001-013 has the manual testing playbook scenario its phase wrote, give each a feature catalog entry, and close the program with both alignment checks reporting no open finding.

### Decisions

Frozen choices. Changing one is an amendment.

| ID | Decision |
|----|----------|
| D1 | Each implementation phase writes its own playbook scenarios through `/create:manual-testing-playbook` (operator decision 2026-11-17); this phase only verifies coverage and routes a gap to its owning phase. |
| D2 | Catalog entries are written only through `/create:feature-catalog`; other doc findings are fixed here by direct edit, and a compiled contract they make stale is regenerated, never hand-edited. |
| D3 | This phase changes docs only; a code finding goes back to its owning phase and blocks closure until fixed or waived by the operator. |
| D4 | This phase starts only after phases 001-013 are complete. |

<!-- /ANCHOR:directive -->

## Known Limitations

Its second run has one open finding, a YAML reference handed to the coordinator. Goal criteria 5 and 6 stay open.
3. **No phase commit exists.** The `git diff` part of criterion 6 cannot be evaluated.
4. **Expansive mode is documented from code and tests.** No live pilot result is recorded as proof.
5. **Fifteen catalog leaves were edited directly.** Decision D2 routes catalog writes through `/create:feature-catalog`. The leaves got a template separator by a direct edit, and the update YAML was not run. The operator must approve this deviation. The YAML gap that let them ship is that `step_5_validation` runs `validate_document.py` only on the root catalog.

[0m$ [0msed -n '2570,2620p;2735,2790p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs
].join('\n');
      }
    }
  }

  // Under expansive depth mode an open frontier node outranks the continuity
  // focus; the pivot focus and blocked-stop recovery above still come first.
  if (registry.frontier) {
    const frontierFocus = pickFrontierFocus(registry.frontier, iterationRecords);
    if (frontierFocus) return frontierFocus;
  }

  const filteredInputs = filterNextFocusInputs(registry, iterationFiles, iterationRecords);
  // A leaf recommendation goes through the same rejected-pattern suppression as every other next-focus candidate.
  const latestLeafNextFocus = [...(Array.isArray(filteredInputs.iterationFiles) ? filteredInputs.iterationFiles : [])]
    .sort((left, right) => (left.run || 0) - (right.run || 0))
    .map((iteration) => iteration.nextFocus)
    .filter((value) => typeof value === 'string' && value.trim())
    .filter((value) => !findRejectedPatternMatch({ text: value, category: 'next-focus' }, rejectedIndex, { category: 'next-focus' }))
    .at(-1) || '';
  const continuityFocus = deriveNextFocusFromContinuity({
    iterationFiles: filteredInputs.iterationFiles,
    iterationRecords: filteredInputs.iterationRecords,
    carriedForwardOpenQuestions: filteredInputs.carriedForwardOpenQuestions,
    machineOpenQuestions: filteredInputs.openQuestions,
    leafNextFocus: latestLeafNextFocus,
    resolvedQuestions: Array.isArray(registry.resolvedQuestions) ? registry.resolvedQuestions : [],
  });

  const normalizedFocus = normalizeOptionalText(continuityFocus).toLowerCase();
  const shouldUseIdeaFocus = !normalizedFocus
    || normalizedFocus === '[none yet]'
    || normalizedFocus === '[all tracked questions are resolved]';
  if (!shouldUseIdeaFocus) {
    return continuityFocus;
  }

  const promotedIdea = Array.isArray(registry.promotedIdeas)
    ? registry.promotedIdeas[0]
    : null;
  return promotedIdea?.idea || continuityFocus;
}

function buildRegistry(strategyQuestions, iterationFiles, iterationRecords, eventRecords, reducerState = {}) {
  const questionCoverageRecords = iterationRecords.filter((record) => {
    const status = normalizeText(String(record.status || 'complete')).toLowerCase();

  });

    rejectedIndex: rejectedPatternIndex,
  });
  const graphConvergence = buildGraphConvergenceRollup(eventRecords);
  const divergence = buildPivotState(eventRecords);
  const lineage = reducerState.lineage && typeof reducerState.lineage === 'object'
    ? reducerState.lineage
    : {};
  const openQuestions = keyedQuestions.filter((question) => !question.resolved).map((question) => ({
    ...buildQuestionFields({

      resolvedAtIteration: null,
    }),
  }));
  const questionConflicts = Array.isArray(reducerState.questionConflicts)
    ? reducerState.questionConflicts
    : [];

    iterationFiles,
    iterationRecords,
    machineOpenQuestions: openQuestions,
    registryQuestions: keyedQuestions,
    resolvedQuestions: keyedQuestions.filter((question) => question.resolved),
  });
  const suppressedCandidates = buildSuppressedNextFocusCandidates({
    openQuestions,
    carriedForwardOpenQuestions,
    promotedIdeas: ideaBacklog.promotedIdeas,
    iterationFiles,
    iterationRecords,
    blockedStopHistory,
    rejectedIndex: rejectedPatternIndex,
  });
  const uncoveredQuestions = keyedQuestions.filter((question) => !question.resolved).map((question) => question.text);

  return {
    sessionId: lineage.sessionId || '',
    parentSessionId: lineage.parentSessionId ?? null,
    lineageMode: lineage.lineageMode || 'new',
    generation: lineage.generation ?? 1,
    continuedFromRun: lineage.continuedFromRun ?? null,
    terminalStop: reducerState.terminalStop || null,
    keyQuestions: keyedQuestions.map((question) => ({

      resolved: question.resolved,
    })),
    openQuestions,
    resolvedQuestions: keyedQuestions.filter((question) => question.resolved).map((question) => ({
      ...buildQuestionFields(question),
    })),
    carriedForwardOpenQuestions,
    uncoveredQuestions,

/** Build and validate the isolated artifact paths for one pivot Council transaction. */
export function buildPivotCouncilPaths(
  artifactRoot: string,
  pivotId: string,
  attempt: PivotAttempt = 1,
): PivotCouncilPaths {
  if (attempt !== 1 && attempt !== 2) {
    throw new TypeError('attempt must be 1 or 2.');
  }
  const rootInput = nonEmptyString(artifactRoot, 'artifactRoot');
  if (!/^pivot-[1-9][0-9]*-[a-f0-9]{12}$/u.test(pivotId)) {
    throw new TypeError('pivotId must match pivot-<ordinal>-<12 lowercase hex characters>.');
  }
  const canonicalRoot = resolveProspectiveRealPath(rootInput);
  if (pathContainsAiCouncil(canonicalRoot)) {
    throw new TypeError('artifactRoot must not be inside an ai-council artifact tree.');
  }
  const pivotRoot = join(canonicalRoot, 'divergent', 'pivots', pivotId);
  const councilRoot = join(pivotRoot, 'council');
  const attemptRoot = attempt === 2 ? join(councilRoot, 'attempt-2') : councilRoot;
  const seatsRoot = join(attemptRoot, 'seats');
  return {
    artifactRoot: canonicalRoot,
    pivotRoot,
    councilRoot,
    configPath: join(councilRoot, 'config.json'),
    statePath: join(councilRoot, 'state.jsonl'),
    seatsRoot,
    seatPaths: [
      join(seatsRoot, 'seat-001.md'),
      join(seatsRoot, 'seat-002.md'),
      join(seatsRoot, 'seat-003.md'),
    ],
    deliberationPath: join(attemptRoot, 'deliberation.md'),
    reportPath: join(councilRoot, 'report.md'),
  };
}

/** Parse and validate an injected seat output without accepting partial reports. */
export function parsePivotSeatReport(
  output: unknown,
  expectedSeatId: string,
  candidateIds: ReadonlySet<string>,
): PivotSeatReportParseResult {
  let parsed = output;
  if (typeof parsed === 'string') {
    try {
      parsed = JSON.parse(parsed);
    } catch {
      return { valid: false, reason: 'Seat output must be a JSON object or JSON object string.' };
    }
  }
  if (!isRecord(parsed)) {
    return { valid: false, reason: 'Seat output must be an object.' };
  }
  if (parsed.seatId !== expectedSeatId) {
    return { valid: false, reason: `Seat report must identify itself as "${expectedSeatId}".` };
  }
  const selectedCandidateId = parsed.selectedCandidateId;
  if (selectedCandidateId !== null
    && (typeof selectedCandidateId !== 'string' || !candidateIds.has(selectedCandidateId))) {
    return { valid: false, reason: 'selectedCandidateId must be null or identify a valid candidate.' };
  }
  if (typeof parsed.materialEndorsement !== 'boolean') {
    return { valid: false, reason: 'materialEndorsement must be a boolean.' };
  }
  if (parsed.materialEndorsement && selectedCandidateId === null) {
    return { valid: false, reason: 'A material endorsement must identify a selected candidate.' };
  }
  if (typeof parsed.rationale !== 'string' || parsed.rationale.trim() === '') {
    return { valid: false, reason: 'Seat rationale must be a non-empty string.' };
  }
  if (!Array.isArray(parsed.blockers)) {
    return { valid: false, reason: 'Seat blockers must be an array.' };
  }

<!-- SPECKIT_TEMPLATE_SOURCE: spec-core | v2.4 -->
# Feature Specification: Divergent pivot hygiene: seat cap scope, dead minRemainingIterations guard, one council retry

<!-- SPECKIT_LEVEL: 1 -->
---

<!-- ANCHOR:metadata -->
## 1. METADATA

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Level** | 1 |
| **Priority** | P2 |
| **Status** | Planned |
| **Created** | 2026-11-17 |
| **Branch** | `main` |
| **Parent Spec** | `../spec.md` |
| **Predecessor** | None. This slice ships in any order with the other defect slices. |
| **Successor** | `../010-research-frontier-mode/` and `../011-review-frontier-mode/`. Both gate the pivot branch this slice rewrites for the retry (research `deep-research.yaml:1056-1112`, review `deep-review.yaml:1158-1206`), and 011 also edits `divergent-review-pivot.ts`, so land this slice before either. |
| **Source** | Parent `research/research.md` §12 item 4, §18 pivot yield, Eliminated Alternatives (`minRemainingIterations`), §20 Slice L; plan review `../scratch/fable-5-2-plan-review.md` F012-1 to F012-3, X-1, X-4, X-7, X-9, F014-2 |
<!-- /ANCHOR:metadata -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:problem -->
## 2. PROBLEM & PURPOSE

### Problem Statement
The divergent pivot (`runtime/lib/deep-loop/divergent-pivot.ts`) has three hygiene gaps. `maxCouncilSeatOutputs: 9` is a lifetime cap that three pivots of three seats fill exactly, so a pivot that is resumed or fails still consumes seats and shrinks every later pivot. `minRemainingIterations: 1` is checked at :739 but can never fire, because a run with no iterations left is stopped by the `maxIterationsReached` cap at `convergence.cjs:1115` before the pivot path runs. And a council quorum failure (`council_all_seats_rejected`, `council_return_quorum_failed`, `council_parse_quorum_failed`) fails closed with no retry, so one transient dispatch problem ends the pivot.

### Purpose
The seat cap has the scope the operator chose, the dead guard no longer pretends to protect anything, and a transient council quorum failure gets exactly one retry inside the seat budget.
<!-- /ANCHOR:problem -->

<!-- ANCHOR:measured-behavior -->
### Measured Behavior
Run from `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.
```text
$ rg -n "maxCouncilSeatOutputs: 9|minRemainingIterations: 1|councilSeatOutputs \+ 3 >|remainingIterations < config" lib/deep-loop/divergent-pivot.ts
286:  maxCouncilSeatOutputs: 9,
287:  minRemainingIterations: 1,
736:  if (usage.councilSeatOutputs + 3 > config.maxCouncilSeatOutputs) {
739:  if (usage.remainingIterations < config.minRemainingIterations) {
$ rg -n "iterationCount >= " scripts/convergence.cjs
1115:  if (iterationCount >= maxIterations) {
```
The seat check adds 3 per pivot against a total of 9. The remaining-iterations check sits behind the cap at :1115, which stops the run first.
<!-- /ANCHOR:measured-behavior -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:scope -->
## 3. SCOPE

### In Scope
- Record the operator's choices on the seat-cap scope in `decision-record.md`: 3 seat outputs per council attempt, at most one retry per pivot, so at most 6 per pivot (decided 2026-11-17).
- Apply that scope in the limit check at `divergent-pivot.ts:736`, in how usage is accumulated (:1485-1486), and where usage is counted: `divergent-research-pivot.ts:420` and `divergent-review-pivot.ts:405` count every `pivot_seat_returned` event the run ever wrote (`readPivotEvents(artifactRoot)`), a lifetime number. Both counts are scoped to the current pivot id and attempt.
- Stop enforcing `minRemainingIterations` while still accepting it in configs and persisted pivot limits (:463, :1025-1032).
- Retry the council round once on a quorum failure, never on `council_agreement_failed`, with an attempt number (1 or 2) carried in event ids, seat output paths and the persisted pivot config.
- Regenerate both compiled contracts, add a changelog entry for the new seat-cap meaning and a manual testing playbook scenario for the retry.

### Out of Scope
- `maxPivots` and its default of 3.
- Council seat mandates and candidate seeding. Pivot yield is bound by council agreement, which a retry does not change.
- Making `minRemainingIterations` reachable by redefining it. That would change pivot behavior near the cap, which no finding asks for.

### Files to Change

| File Path | Change Type | Description |
|-----------|-------------|-------------|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/divergent-pivot.ts` | Modify | Seat-cap scope per ADR-001 (:286, :736, :1485-1486); drop the :739 check while keeping the field readable; let finalize report a quorum failure as retryable once and let prepare open a second attempt for the same pivot. The attempt needs an identity everywhere the code fixes one today: seat event ids (`seatEventId`, :600-601), the `pivot_failed` and `pivot_deliberation_completed` ids (:1295, :1423, :1441), `eventByName` (:596-598, returns the first match), seat output paths (`buildPivotCouncilPaths`, :807-830, fixed `seat-001.md` to `seat-003.md`) and the persisted config check `invariants.rounds === 1` (:1130-1165). Seat ids stay `seat-001` to `seat-003`, because `REQUIRED_SEAT_IDS` (:283) is enforced by index (:500-501) |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/divergent-pivot.cjs` | Modify | Carry the retry signal and the attempt number through the `prepare` and `finalize` subcommands, the workflow-facing seam |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/divergent-research-pivot.ts` | Modify | Scope the seat count at :420 to the current pivot id and attempt. `../010-research-frontier-mode/` may also edit this file |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/scripts/divergent-review-pivot.ts` | Modify | Scope the seat count at :405 to the current pivot id and attempt. `../011-review-frontier-mode/` also edits this file |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Modify | In the pivot failure branch after `step_finalize_divergent_pivot` (:1112-1207), go back to `step_prepare_divergent_pivot` once when finalize marks the failure retryable |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Modify | The same one-time re-entry after its `step_finalize_divergent_pivot` (:1206) |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/compiled/deep-research.contract.markdown`, `deep-review.contract.markdown` | Regenerate | Both YAMLs are digest sources: `node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/compile-command-contracts.cjs --command deep/research --write` and `--command deep/review --write` |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/` | Create | A changelog entry stating the new meaning of `maxCouncilSeatOutputs` (3 per council attempt) and the one retry |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/manual-testing-playbook/council/` | Modify | A retry scenario through `/create:manual-testing-playbook system-deep-loop update` |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/divergent-pivot.vitest.ts` | Modify | Cases for the seat scope, the retry (attempt-2 event ids and seat paths do not collide with attempt 1), the persisted-limits read and the scoped seat count |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/council/multi-seat-dispatch.vitest.ts` | Baseline only | Unchanged. It tests `dispatchCouncilSeats` with any seat ids; the workflow, not that module, drives the pivot's seats, so a retry case there would not touch the changed code |
<!-- /ANCHOR:scope -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:requirements -->
## 4. REQUIREMENTS

REQ ids run in one sequence across the P0 and P1 tables. A new requirement takes the highest REQ id plus one. validate.sh does not link ids between files.

### P0 - Blockers (MUST complete)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance Criteria |
|----|-------------|---------------------|
| REQ-001 | The operator's seat-cap choice is recorded before the cap changes. | `decision-record.md` ADR-001 shows `Accepted` and names 3 seat outputs per council attempt with at most one retry per pivot; OD-001 and OD-002 carry the decision. |
| REQ-002 | A council quorum failure is retried once, and the retry attempt gets its own 3 seat outputs. | A `divergent-pivot.vitest.ts` case: first attempt returns 2 of 3 seats, the retry attempt returns 3 valid seats; the pivot completes and records 6 seat outputs. A second consecutive quorum failure fails closed with the original reason, and no third attempt is opened. |
| REQ-003 | `council_agreement_failed` is not retried. | A case where three valid seats disagree fails closed after one round with `council_agreement_failed` and records 3 seat outputs. |
| REQ-004 | Persisted pivot limits that carry `minRemainingIterations` still load. | A case reading a persisted config with `minRemainingIterations: 1` parses without error, and no pivot is refused with `insufficient_remaining_iterations`. |

### P1 - Required (complete OR user-approved deferral)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance Criteria |
|----|-------------|---------------------|
| REQ-005 | The seat cap follows ADR-001: 3 seat outputs per council attempt, at most one retry per pivot. | A case where pivot 1 used 6 seats (with retry) still allows pivot 2, no pivot records more than 6 seat outputs, and resuming an open attempt that holds 2 seat returns is not refused by the cap. |
| REQ-006 | Default runs are unaffected. | `divergent-pivot.vitest.ts`, `divergent-pivot-cli.vitest.ts` and `multi-seat-dispatch.vitest.ts` pass; the divergent path runs only under `--convergence-mode divergent`. |
| REQ-007 | The retry attempt has its own identity. | A `divergent-pivot.vitest.ts` case: after a retried pivot, the state log holds attempt-1 and attempt-2 seat events with distinct event ids, both attempts' seat files exist, the persisted config parses, and a pivot written before this change (attempt 1 ids, `invariants.rounds: 1`) still loads. A first-attempt quorum failure writes no terminal `pivot_failed`. |
| REQ-008 | The seat count is scoped. | A case with two pivots in one artifact root: the usage that `divergent-research-pivot.ts` and `divergent-review-pivot.ts` pass for pivot 2 counts 0 seat outputs, not pivot 1's. |
| REQ-009 | Generated and documented surfaces stay in step. | `check-contract-drift.vitest.ts` and `compile-command-contracts.vitest.ts` pass after both contracts are regenerated; a changelog entry and a council playbook retry scenario exist. |
<!-- /ANCHOR:requirements -->

## 5. SUCCESS CRITERIA

- **SC-001**: `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run tests/integration/divergent-pivot.vitest.ts tests/council/multi-seat-dispatch.vitest.ts tests/unit/divergent-pivot-cli.vitest.ts` passes, including the new cases.
- **SC-002**: `rg -n "insufficient_remaining_iterations" lib/deep-loop/divergent-pivot.ts` shows the reason kept only in the reason set for reading older events, with no live check producing it.
<!-- /ANCHOR:success-criteria -->

<!-- ANCHOR:risks -->
## 6. RISKS & DEPENDENCIES

| Type | Item | Impact | Mitigation |
|------|------|--------|------------|
| Risk | The seat round is driven by the workflow, not by code: the YAML runs prepare, three seat dispatches, record-seat and finalize. A retry built only in `divergent-pivot.ts` would never run. | Med | The retry is a one-time YAML re-entry into prepare, signalled by finalize; both YAMLs change together |
| Dependency | Operator decision on parent §12 item 4 | None now: ADR-001 is Accepted (OD-001, OD-002), so no task waits on it | T001 is done |
| Dependency | `../010-research-frontier-mode/` and `../011-review-frontier-mode/` change the same pivot branches and pivot scripts | Same-function conflicts in both YAMLs and both pivot scripts | Land this slice first; regenerate both compiled contracts after each merge |
| Risk | Default-path risk is LOW: the pivot runs only in `divergent` convergence mode, which is opt-in (`.skilled/commands/deep/research.md:103`). | Low | REQ-006 suites |
| Risk | Each retry costs three more seat outputs, so a run's worst-case seat cost rises from 9 to 18. | Low | The retry fires only on a quorum failure and at most once per pivot (ADR-001) |
| Risk | Removing the field outright would break reads of persisted pivot limits, which list it as required (:1025). | Med | Keep the field accepted on read; only the check is dropped (REQ-004) |
| Risk | `prepare` reruns the preflight cap check on a resume (`divergent-pivot.ts:1244`, :1284), so a per-attempt count would refuse a resumed attempt that already holds seat returns | High if missed | Run the seat-cap check only when an attempt is opened; a REQ-005 case resumes an attempt with 2 seat returns |
| Risk | Attempt 2 reuses the fixed ids and paths: `${pivotId}:pivot_seat_returned:${seatId}`, `seats/seat-00N.md`, `${pivotId}:pivot_failed`, and `eventByName` returns the first match, so attempt 2 would read attempt 1's events | High if missed | Carry the attempt number in every id and path; keep attempt-1 ids unchanged so older pivots load (REQ-007) |
<!-- /ANCHOR:risks -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:questions -->
## 7. OPEN QUESTIONS

- Answered 2026-11-17: `maxCouncilSeatOutputs` is 3 per council attempt, and the one retry per pivot gets its own 3, so one pivot uses at most 6. See `decision-record.md` ADR-001.
- Narrowing note: parent §20 Slice L says one retry for `council_*` refusals. This phase retries only the three quorum failures, because `council_agreement_failed` is a substantive verdict a re-run would only repeat at three more seats. The operator can widen it in ADR-001.
<!-- /ANCHOR:questions -->

function persistConfig(path: string, config: Record<string, unknown>): void {
  if (existsSync(path)) {
    const existing = JSON.parse(readFileSync(path, 'utf8')) as unknown;
    if (stableJson(existing) !== stableJson(config)) {
      throw new Error('Existing pivot config conflicts with the resumed transaction.');
    }
    return;
  }
  writeStateIfChangedAtomic(path, config, new Map<string, string>());
}

function writeMarkdownIfConsistent(path: string, content: string): void {
  if (existsSync(path)) {
    if (readFileSync(path, 'utf8') !== content) {
      throw new Error(`Existing pivot artifact conflicts with durable state: ${path}`);
    }
    return;

+// The frontier queue only exists under the expansive depth mode, so a run that
+// never asked for it projects no nodes and every frontier-aware branch below
+// stays on its default path.
+function expansiveFrontierNodes(config: JsonRecord, registry: JsonRecord): JsonRecord[] {
+  const antiConvergence = isRecord(config.antiConvergence) ? config.antiConvergence : {};
+  if (antiConvergence.depthMode !== 'expansive') return [];
+  const frontier = isRecord(registry.frontier) ? registry.frontier : {};
+  return Array.isArray(frontier.nodes) ? frontier.nodes.filter(isRecord) : [];
+}

+// The frontier node store is append-only and the last row per node wins, so a
+// reduce rebuilds the same node list every time it reads the same file. The
+// history keeps every valid row in file order: a status a node has since left
+// still has to attribute the iterations it spent there.
+function readReviewFrontierStore(storePath) {
+  if (!fs.existsSync(storePath)) return { nodes: [], history: [] };
+  const byId = new Map();
+  const history = [];
+  for (const line of readUtf8(storePath).split('\n')) {
+    if (!line.trim()) continue;
+    let row;
+    try { row = JSON.parse(line); } catch { continue; }
+    if (!row || typeof row !== 'object' || typeof row.nodeId !== 'string' || !row.nodeId) continue;
+    byId.set(row.nodeId, row);
+    history.push(row);
+  }
+  return {
+    nodes: [...byId.values()].sort((left, right) => left.nodeId.localeCompare(right.nodeId)),
+    history,
+  };
+}
+
+// A focus carries the node it was dispatched to as a marker phrase, and a bare
+// substring match would let a node claim a longer id that starts with its own,
+// so the id is bounded on both sides.
+function frontierMarkerPattern(nodeId) {
+  return new RegExp(`(?:^|[^\\w-])Frontier node ${escapeRegExp(String(nodeId))}(?![\\w-])`);
+}
+
+// A leaf can reword a focus before it reaches the state log, so the marker alone
+// cannot attribute every visit. A store row that marked the node active on an
+// iteration's number claims that iteration's record either way.
+function reviewRecordsForNode(nodeId, history, iterationRecords) {
+  const marker = frontierMarkerPattern(nodeId);
+  const activeIterations = new Set(
+    (Array.isArray(history) ? history : [])
+      .filter((row) => row && row.nodeId === nodeId && row.status === 'active')
+      .map((row) => getIterationRun(row))
+      .filter((iteration) => iteration !== null),
+  );
+  return (Array.isArray(iterationRecords) ? iterationRecords : []).filter((record) => {
+    if (!record || record.type !== 'iteration') return false;
+    if (record.frontierNodeId === nodeId) return true;
+    if (typeof record.focus === 'string' && marker.test(record.focus)) return true;
+    const iteration = getIterationRun(record);
+    return iteration !== null && activeIterations.has(iteration);
+  });
+}
+
+// Only an attempt that produced evidence counts as a visit: an errored or timed
+// out record is retried work, not depth the node has already spent.
+function reviewFrontierVisits(nodeId, history, iterationRecords) {
+  return reviewRecordsForNode(nodeId, history, iterationRecords)
+    .filter((record) => !FRONTIER_FAILED_STATUSES.has(String(record.status || '').toLowerCase()))
+    .length;
+}
+
+// Depth bands share maxIterations 50/35/15 by default (depths 0-1, 2, 3). A band's
+// unspent share rolls down to the next band only once the band has no open node,
+// and never up. When a band's spend reaches its share, its open nodes close as
+// exhausted with reason budget, so the run stops waiting on work it cannot fund.
+function applyReviewFrontierBudget(nodes, history, iterationRecords, maxIterations, budgetSplit) {
+  const split = budgetSplit && typeof budgetSplit === 'object' ? budgetSplit : {};
+  const shares = [
+    Number.isFinite(split.depth1) ? split.depth1 : 0.5,
+    Number.isFinite(split.depth2) ? split.depth2 : 0.35,
+    Number.isFinite(split.depth3) ? split.depth3 : 0.15,
+  ];
+  const cap = Number.isFinite(maxIterations) && maxIterations > 0 ? maxIterations : 10;
+  const bandOf = (depth) => (depth <= 1 ? 0 : depth === 2 ? 1 : 2);
+  const spent = [0, 0, 0];
+  for (const node of nodes) {
+    spent[bandOf(node.depth)] += reviewFrontierVisits(node.nodeId, history, iterationRecords);
+  }
+  let result = nodes.map((node) => ({ ...node }));
+  const budget = {};
+  let carry = 0;
+  for (let band = 0; band < 3; band += 1) {
+    const allotted = cap * shares[band] + carry;
+    if (spent[band] >= allotted) {
+      result = result.map((node) => (bandOf(node.depth) === band && (node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active')
+        ? { ...node, status: 'exhausted', reason: 'budget' }
+        : node));
+    }
+    const bandOpen = result.some((node) => bandOf(node.depth) === band && (node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active'));
+    carry = bandOpen ? 0 : Math.max(0, allotted - spent[band]);
+    budget[`depth${band + 1}`] = { allotted: Math.round(allotted * 100) / 100, spent: spent[band] };
+  }
+  return { nodes: result, budget };
+}
+
+// A node is answered once every dimension in the review's universe has evidence
+// behind it. That status is derived on every reduce instead of written back, so
+// the store keeps only the lifecycle its writer owns and the projection stays a
+// pure function of the rows it read.
+function buildReviewFrontier(storeData, iterationRecords, config) {
+  const universe = buildDimensionUniverse(config);
+  const history = Array.isArray(storeData?.history) ? storeData.history : [];
+  const storedNodes = Array.isArray(storeData?.nodes) ? storeData.nodes : [];
+  const nodes = storedNodes.map((node) => {
+    const nodeId = node.nodeId;
+    const records = reviewRecordsForNode(nodeId, history, iterationRecords)
+      .filter((record) => !FRONTIER_FAILED_STATUSES.has(String(record.status || '').toLowerCase()));
+    const covered = new Set();
+    for (const record of records) {
+      if (!Array.isArray(record.dimensions)) continue;
+      for (const dimension of record.dimensions) {
+        const name = String(dimension).trim().toLowerCase();
+        if (name) covered.add(name);
+      }
+    }
+    const coveredDimensions = universe.filter((dimension) => covered.has(dimension));
+    const stillOpen = node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active';
+    const status = stillOpen && coveredDimensions.length === universe.length ? 'answered' : node.status;
+    return {
+      nodeId,
+      parentId: node.parentId ?? null,
+      depth: node.depth,
+      scope: node.scope ?? null,
+      question: node.question ?? null,
+      status,
+      reason: status === node.status ? (node.reason ?? null) : null,
+      iteration: node.iteration ?? null,
+      coveredDimensions,
+      nextDimension: universe.find((dimension) => !covered.has(dimension)) ?? null,
+      visits: records.length,
+    };
+  });
+  const budgeted = applyReviewFrontierBudget(
+    nodes,
+    history,
+    iterationRecords,
+    Number(config?.maxIterations),
+    config?.expansiveDepth?.budgetSplit,
+  );
+  const projected = budgeted.nodes;
+  const stats = { total: projected.length };
+  for (const status of FRONTIER_STATUSES) stats[status] = projected.filter((node) => node.status === status).length;
+  // Breadth first: the shallowest node, then one not yet visited, then the oldest.
+  const chosen = projected
+    .filter((node) => node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active')
+    .sort((left, right) => ((left.depth ?? 0) - (right.depth ?? 0))
+      || (left.visits - right.visits)
+      || ((left.iteration ?? 0) - (right.iteration ?? 0))
+      || left.nodeId.localeCompare(right.nodeId))[0] ?? null;
+  return {
+    nodes: projected,
+    stats,
+    budget: budgeted.budget,
+    activeNodeId: chosen ? chosen.nodeId : null,
+    activeNextDimension: chosen ? chosen.nextDimension : null,
+  };
+}
+
+function frontierScopeRefs(scope) {
+  if (Array.isArray(scope)) return asStringList(scope);
+  const single = normalizeText(scope);
+  return single ? [single] : [];
+}
+
+// A node the store still holds open is search depth the run has not spent yet,
+// so it belongs in the same debt ledger a leaf's own deferral lands in. Exhausted
+// and blocked nodes stay frontier-only: their question is closed, and filing them
+// as debt would report work the run is no longer allowed to do.
+function mergeFrontierIntoSearchLedger(searchLedgerState, frontier) {
+  const candidateCoverage = searchLedgerState.candidateCoverage;
+  const debtById = new Map(
+    (Array.isArray(searchLedgerState.searchDebt) ? searchLedgerState.searchDebt : [])
+      .map((entry) => [entry.id, entry]),
+  );
+  for (const node of frontier.nodes) {
+    const bugClass = `frontier:${node.nodeId}`;
+    const iteration = node.iteration ?? 0;
+    if (node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active') {
+      const id = `frontier-${node.nodeId}`;
+      debtById.set(id, {
+        id,
+        iteration,
+        targetRefs: frontierScopeRefs(node.scope),
+        bugClass,
+        disposition: 'deferred',
+        rationale: `Frontier node ${node.nodeId} is still open: ${node.question}`,
+        evidenceRefs: [`frontier-nodes.jsonl#${node.nodeId}`],
+        reason: 'frontier node open',
+        frontier: true,
+      });
+      addCandidateClass(candidateCoverage, bugClass, 'deferred', iteration);
+    } else if (node.status === 'answered') {
+      addCandidateClass(candidateCoverage, bugClass, 'covered', iteration);
+    } else if (node.status === 'pruned' || node.status === 'abstained') {
+      addCandidateClass(candidateCoverage, bugClass, 'ruled_out', iteration);
+    }
+  }
+  searchLedgerState.searchDebt = [...debtById.values()]
+    .sort((left, right) => left.iteration - right.iteration || left.id.localeCompare(right.id));
+  for (const key of ['covered', 'ruledOut', 'deferred', 'blocked']) {
+    candidateCoverage[key].sort((left, right) => left.localeCompare(right));
+  }
+  for (const state of Object.values(candidateCoverage.byBugClass)) {
+    state.iterations.sort((left, right) => Number(left) - Number(right));
+  }
+  return searchLedgerState;
+}
+

+// A free-text leaf focus must not hide a required dimension the run has not covered yet.
+function pickReviewBaseFocus(iterationFiles, registry) {
+  const leafFocus = iterationFiles.map((iteration) => iteration.nextFocus).filter(Boolean).at(-1) || '';
+  const uncovered = Object.keys(registry.dimensionCoverage).filter((dimension) => !registry.dimensionCoverage[dimension]);
+  const leafKey = String(leafFocus).toLowerCase().trim();
+  if (leafFocus && (uncovered.length === 0 || uncovered.some((dimension) => leafKey.includes(dimension)))) {
+    return leafFocus;
+  }
+  return uncovered[0] || leafFocus || '[All dimensions covered]';
+}
+
+// The expansive frontier is the run's own queue, and the node it chose carries
+// the dimension that node still owes, so a leaf's free-text focus cannot point
+// the next iteration at work the frontier has already closed or reordered.
+function pickReviewFrontierFocus(frontier) {
+  if (!frontier || typeof frontier !== 'object' || !frontier.activeNodeId) return '';
+  const nodes = Array.isArray(frontier.nodes) ? frontier.nodes : [];
+  const node = nodes.find((candidate) => candidate && candidate.nodeId === frontier.activeNodeId);
+  if (!node) return '';
+  const scope = Array.isArray(node.scope) ? node.scope.join(', ') : (node.scope || 'unscoped');
+  return `Frontier node ${node.nodeId} (depth ${node.depth}, scope ${scope}): ${node.question} - review the ${frontier.activeNextDimension} dimension`;
+}

+// The frontier metrics only exist once the expansive depth mode projects a
+// frontier, so a default run renders no section at all rather than an empty one.
+function renderFrontierSection(frontier) {
+  if (!frontier || typeof frontier !== 'object') return [];
+  const stats = frontier.stats || {};
+  const nodes = Array.isArray(frontier.nodes) ? frontier.nodes : [];
+  // A visit that covered no new dimension is depth spent again on ground the
+  // run had already walked, which the size and status counts cannot show.
+  const revisits = nodes.reduce((total, node) => {
+    const visits = Number(node?.visits) || 0;
+    const covered = Array.isArray(node?.coveredDimensions) ? node.coveredDimensions.length : 0;
+    return total + Math.max(0, visits - covered);
+  }, 0);
+  const budgetEntries = Object.entries(
+    frontier.budget && typeof frontier.budget === 'object' ? frontier.budget : {},
+  );
+  return [
+    '<!-- ANCHOR:frontier -->',
+    '## 10B. FRONTIER',
+    `- Frontier size: ${stats.total} nodes (${stats.open} open, ${stats.active} active, ${stats.answered} answered, ${stats.exhausted} exhausted, ${stats.blocked} blocked)`,
+    `- Pruned nodes: ${stats.pruned}`,
+    `- Revisits: ${revisits}`,
+    ...(budgetEntries.length
+      ? budgetEntries.map(([band, entry]) => `- Budget ${band}: ${entry.spent} of ${entry.allotted} iterations spent`)
+      : ['- Budget: not recorded']),
+    ...(frontier.activeNodeId
+      ? [`- Active node: ${frontier.activeNodeId} (${frontier.activeNextDimension})`]
+      : ['- Active node: none']),
+    '',
+    '<!-- /ANCHOR:frontier -->',
+  ];
+}

+const FRONTIER_STATUSES = ['open', 'active', 'answered', 'blocked', 'exhausted', 'abstained', 'pruned'];

+// Lineages number the nodes they admit from their own store, so two lineages
+// that each hold one dealt node both name their first child F-2-002. A merged
+// node id is therefore qualified by its lineage label, its parent link with it,
+// and the lineage's own id stays beside it. The partition guard reads the depth-1
+// split the fan-out runner recorded against those lineage ids: a node dealt to a
+// lineage that no lineage reports was lost, and the guard names it instead of
+// letting the merged frontier look complete.
+function qualifyFrontierId(label, nodeId) {
+  return typeof nodeId === 'string' && nodeId !== '' && nodeId !== 'root' ? `${label}:${nodeId}` : nodeId;
+}
+
+function mergeResearchFrontier(lineageData, frontierPartition) {
+  const nodes = [];
+  for (const { label, registry } of [...lineageData].sort((left, right) => compareText(left.label, right.label))) {
+    const lineageNodes = Array.isArray(registry?.frontier?.nodes) ? registry.frontier.nodes : [];
+    for (const node of lineageNodes) {
+      nodes.push({
+        ...node,
+        nodeId: qualifyFrontierId(label, node.nodeId),
+        lineageNodeId: node.nodeId,
+        ...(Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(node, 'parentId') ? { parentId: qualifyFrontierId(label, node.parentId) } : {}),
+        lineage: label,
+      });
+    }
+  }
+  nodes.sort((left, right) => compareText(left.lineage, right.lineage) || compareText(left.lineageNodeId, right.lineageNodeId));
+  const stats = { total: nodes.length };
+  for (const status of FRONTIER_STATUSES) stats[status] = nodes.filter((node) => node.status === status).length;
+  const reported = new Set(nodes.map((node) => node.lineageNodeId));
+  const expected = frontierPartition && typeof frontierPartition === 'object'
+    ? Object.values(frontierPartition).flatMap((ids) => (Array.isArray(ids) ? ids : []))
+    : [];
+  const missing = [...new Set(expected.filter((id) => !reported.has(id)))].sort(compareText);
+  return {
+    nodes,
+    stats,
+    ...(expected.length > 0 ? { partitionGuard: { passed: missing.length === 0, missing } } : {}),
+  };
+}

@@ -861,6 +906,157 @@ function mergeResearchRegistries(lineageData, options = {}) {
 // 4. REVIEW MERGE  (strongest-restriction)
 // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 
+// A review lineage records what it covered in six surfaces. Merging them lets a guard
+// read what the fan-out as a whole covered. Every merge below walks lineages in label
+// order and sorts its output, so the merged registry never depends on arrival order.
+const TRACEABILITY_STATUS_RANK = { fail: 5, blocked: 4, partial: 3, pass: 2, notApplicable: 1 };
+const TRACEABILITY_UNDECIDED = new Set(['pending', 'deferred', 'skipped']);
+const COVERAGE_BUCKETS = ['covered', 'ruledOut', 'deferred', 'blocked'];
+
+function compareText(a, b) {
+  const left = String(a);
+  const right = String(b);
+  if (left < right) return -1;
+  return left > right ? 1 : 0;
+}
+
+function sortedUnion(lists) {
+  return [...new Set(lists.flatMap((list) => (Array.isArray(list) ? list : [])))].sort(compareText);
+}
+
+function lineagesByLabel(lineageData) {
+  return lineageData
+    .filter((lineage) => lineage && lineage.registry && typeof lineage.registry === 'object')
+    .sort((a, b) => compareText(a.label, b.label));
+}
+
+// A dimension counts as covered when any lineage covered it.
+function mergeDimensionCoverage(lineages) {
+  const merged = {};
+  for (const { registry } of lineages) {
+    const coverage = registry.dimensionCoverage;
+    if (!coverage || typeof coverage !== 'object') continue;
+    for (const [dimension, covered] of Object.entries(coverage)) {
+      merged[dimension] = merged[dimension] === true || covered === true;
+    }
+  }
+  return Object.fromEntries(Object.keys(merged).sort(compareText).map((key) => [key, merged[key]]));
+}
+
+function traceabilityRank(status) {
+  if (TRACEABILITY_UNDECIDED.has(status)) return 0;
+  return TRACEABILITY_STATUS_RANK[status] ?? 0;
+}
+
+// The strictest status wins per protocol, and an undecided status never replaces a
+// decided one; a tie keeps the first lineage in label order. The summary is then
+// recomputed from the merged results with the reducer's own counting rules.
+function mergeTraceability(lineages) {
+  const byProtocol = new Map();
+  for (const { registry } of lineages) {
+    const results = registry.traceability?.results;
+    if (!Array.isArray(results)) continue;
+    for (const result of results) {
+      if (!result || !result.protocolId) continue;
+      const previous = byProtocol.get(result.protocolId);
+      if (!previous || traceabilityRank(result.status) > traceabilityRank(previous.status)) {
+        byProtocol.set(result.protocolId, result);
+      }
+    }
+  }
+  const results = [...byProtocol.keys()].sort(compareText).map((protocolId) => byProtocol.get(protocolId));
+  const statuses = results.map((result) => result.status);
+  const applicable = results.filter((result) => result.applicable !== false);
+  return {
+    summary: {
+      required: applicable.length,
+      executed: applicable.filter((result) => !TRACEABILITY_UNDECIDED.has(result.status)).length,
+      pass: statuses.filter((status) => status === 'pass').length,
+      partial: statuses.filter((status) => status === 'partial').length,
+      fail: statuses.filter((status) => status === 'fail').length,
+      blocked: statuses.filter((status) => status === 'blocked').length,
+      notApplicable: statuses.filter((status) => status === 'notApplicable').length,
+      gatingFailures: applicable.filter((result) => result.gateClass === 'hard'
+        && ['partial', 'fail', 'blocked'].includes(result.status)).length,
+    },
+    results,
+    unfinishedCoreProtocols: sortedUnion(lineages.map(({ registry }) => registry.traceability?.unfinishedCoreProtocols)),
+  };
+}
+
+function mergeSearchCoverage(lineages) {
+  const coverages = lineages
+    .map(({ registry }) => registry.searchCoverage)
+    .filter((coverage) => coverage && typeof coverage === 'object');
+  const modes = [...new Set(coverages.map((coverage) => coverage.graphCoverageMode ?? null))];
+  return {
+    requiredBugClasses: sortedUnion(coverages.map((coverage) => coverage.requiredBugClasses)),
+    ...Object.fromEntries(COVERAGE_BUCKETS.map((bucket) => [bucket, sortedUnion(coverages.map((coverage) => coverage[bucket]))])),
+    // A mode every lineage shares is still true of the merged run; disagreeing modes are not.
+    graphCoverageMode: modes.length === 1 ? modes[0] : null,
+  };
+}
+
+// A bug class two lineages share has each flag ORed and its iterations unioned; the
+// lineage list keeps those iteration numbers attributable.
+function mergeCandidateCoverage(lineages) {
+  const coverages = [];
+  const byBugClass = {};
+  for (const { label, registry } of lineages) {
+    const coverage = registry.candidateCoverage;
+    if (!coverage || typeof coverage !== 'object') continue;
+    coverages.push(coverage);
+    for (const [bugClass, entry] of Object.entries(coverage.byBugClass || {})) {
+      const merged = byBugClass[bugClass]
+        || { covered: false, ruledOut: false, deferred: false, blocked: false, iterations: [], lineages: [] };
+      for (const bucket of COVERAGE_BUCKETS) merged[bucket] = merged[bucket] || entry?.[bucket] === true;
+      const iterations = Array.isArray(entry?.iterations) ? entry.iterations : [];
+      merged.iterations = [...new Set([...merged.iterations, ...iterations])].sort((a, b) => a - b);
+      merged.lineages = sortedUnion([merged.lineages, [label]]);
+      byBugClass[bugClass] = merged;
+    }
+  }
+  return {
+    ...Object.fromEntries(COVERAGE_BUCKETS.map((bucket) => [bucket, sortedUnion(coverages.map((coverage) => coverage[bucket]))])),
+    byBugClass: Object.fromEntries(Object.keys(byBugClass).sort(compareText).map((key) => [key, byBugClass[key]])),
+  };
+}
+
+// Pivot state belongs to one lineage, and a merged current focus would be invented,
+// so each lineage's divergence is kept whole under its own label.
+function mergeDivergenceByLineage(lineages) {
+  const byLineage = {};
+  for (const { label, registry } of lineages) {
+    if (registry.divergence && typeof registry.divergence === 'object') byLineage[label] = registry.divergence;
+  }
+  return byLineage;
+}
+
+function mergeRuledOutCandidates(lineages) {
+  const byKey = new Map();
+  for (const { label, registry } of lineages) {
+    if (!Array.isArray(registry.ruledOutCandidates)) continue;
+    for (const candidate of registry.ruledOutCandidates) {
+      if (!candidate || !candidate.id) continue;
+      const key = JSON.stringify([label, candidate.id]);
+      if (!byKey.has(key)) byKey.set(key, { ...candidate, lineage: label });
+    }
+  }
+  return [...byKey.values()].sort((a, b) => compareText(a.lineage, b.lineage) || compareText(a.id, b.id));
+}
+
+function mergeReviewCoverageSurfaces(lineageData) {
+  const lineages = lineagesByLabel(lineageData);
+  return {
+    dimensionCoverage: mergeDimensionCoverage(lineages),
+    traceability: mergeTraceability(lineages),
+    searchCoverage: mergeSearchCoverage(lineages),
+    candidateCoverage: mergeCandidateCoverage(lineages),
+    divergenceByLineage: mergeDivergenceByLineage(lineages),
+    ruledOutCandidates: mergeRuledOutCandidates(lineages),
+  };
+}
+

# Fan-out review coverage merge

## 1. OVERVIEW

A review fan-out runs its lineages in parallel and merges their registries into one. The merge carries three coverage surfaces forward from the lineages: candidate coverage, dimension coverage and search coverage.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Merged Surfaces

`runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` `mergeReviewRegistries` merges each lineage's candidate coverage, dimension coverage and search coverage, and adds the merged surfaces to the returned registry.

### Merge Behavior

The surfaces are combined across lineages rather than read from one lineage alone, so the merged registry reflects coverage from every lineage in the fan-out.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` | Script | Merges the review coverage surfaces in `mergeReviewRegistries` and returns them. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/fanout-merge.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Per-surface merge cases. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/fanout-review-focus.vitest.ts` | Vitest | End-to-end merged-coverage case for a review fan-out. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/fanout/fan-out-merged-coverage-surfaces.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-071: a merged two-lineage review keeps its coverage surfaces. |

# DRV-071 -- Fan-out merged review keeps its coverage surfaces

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-071`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the merged coverage surfaces for `DRV-071`. It focuses on a two-lineage review fan-out whose merged registry must carry the coverage that each lineage recorded: `dimensionCoverage`, `traceability`, `searchCoverage`, `candidateCoverage`, `divergenceByLineage` and `ruledOutCandidates`.

### Why This Matters

Before the merge carried these surfaces, the merged review registry dropped the coverage its lineages had recorded. A review that fanned out to two lineages could then show less coverage than either lineage had done, and the convergence guards read the merged registry. Keeping the surfaces is what lets a fan-out review stop on real coverage.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a real check against a two-lineage fan-out artifact, so the merged output is read from the same lineage folders a live fan-out produces.

- Objective: Confirm that `fanout-merge.cjs` returns the six coverage surfaces in the merged review registry, built from both lineages, in deterministic order.
- Real user request: A two-lineage review fan-out finished. Show me the merged coverage, and make sure neither lineage's coverage was dropped in the merge.
- Prompt: `Merge the two-lineage deep-review fan-out artifact and confirm the merged registry keeps dimensionCoverage, traceability, searchCoverage, candidateCoverage, divergenceByLineage and ruledOutCandidates from both lineages.`
- Expected execution process: Read the lineage folders under the fixture artifact directory, run the merge script with `--loop-type review`, and read the six surfaces from its JSON output with `jq`.
- Expected signals: The merge exits 0. `dimensionCoverage` is the union of both lineages with sorted keys. `traceability` keeps the strictest status per protocol, so `fail` beats `blocked`, `blocked` beats `partial`, `partial` beats `pass`, and `pass` beats `notApplicable`. `searchCoverage` and `candidateCoverage` are sorted unions, and each candidate bucket lists its lineages. `divergenceByLineage` holds each lineage's pivot state under its label, with no merged `divergence` key. Each `ruledOutCandidates` entry carries a `lineage` field.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The user is told that the merged review shows coverage from both lineages, with each lineage's divergence kept separate.
- Pass/fail: PASS if all six surfaces are present and built from both lineages, and no merged `divergence` key appears; FAIL if a surface is missing or empty while a lineage recorded it, or the merged output contains a merged `divergence` key.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Merge the two-lineage deep-review fan-out artifact and confirm the merged registry keeps dimensionCoverage, traceability, searchCoverage, candidateCoverage, divergenceByLineage and ruledOutCandidates from both lineages.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n 'mergeReviewCoverageSurfaces|dimensionCoverage|divergenceByLineage' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-merge.vitest fanout-review-focus.vitest`
3. `node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs --loop-type review --artifact-dir <fixture-artifact-dir> | jq '{dimensionCoverage, traceability, searchCoverage, candidateCoverage, divergenceByLineage, ruledOutCandidates}'`
4. `node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs --loop-type review --artifact-dir <fixture-artifact-dir> | jq 'has("divergence")'`

`<fixture-artifact-dir>` holds a `lineages/{label}/` sub-packet for each of the two lineages.

### Expected

Command 1 prints the helper that spreads the six surfaces into the merged registry and the two surface keys. Command 2 exits 0 with no failed test. Command 3 exits 0 and prints the six surfaces, each built from both lineages. Command 4 prints `false`.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 match, the command 2 summary, the command 3 JSON for the six surfaces, and the command 4 output.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: The merge exits 0, all six surfaces are present and built from both lineages, `divergenceByLineage` is keyed by lineage label, and the merged output has no `divergence` key.
- **Fail**: The merge exits non-zero, a surface is missing or empty while a lineage recorded it, the strictest-status rule is broken, or the merged output carries a `divergence` key.

### Failure Triage

If a surface is missing, check that `mergeReviewCoverageSurfaces` is wired into the merged registry in `fanout-merge.cjs`. If the traceability status is wrong, compare the two lineages' per-protocol status first, since the strictest status must win regardless of the order the lineages arrive. If the exit is 3, the artifact directory is missing a lineage sub-packet.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Order: merge the same two lineages with their labels swapped in the directory listing and confirm the output is identical, since every merge walks lineages in label order.

+// The leaf's route proof, gate inputs and telemetry must survive the upcast, or
+// the state log cannot feed the findings gate and the focus guard. A field of
+// the wrong shape is left out rather than defaulted, like an empty focus.
+const LEAF_PROSE_FIELDS = Object.freeze([
+  'target_agent', 'resolved_route', 'noveltyJustification', 'focusTrack', 'edgeCase',
+] as const);
+const LEAF_PROSE_ARRAY_FIELDS = Object.freeze([
+  'keyQuestions', 'toolsUsed', 'sourcesQueried',
+] as const);
+const MAX_LEAF_COUNT = 0xffff_ffff;
+
+function optionalLeafFields(input: Record<string, unknown>): JsonObject {
+  const fields: JsonObject = {};
+  for (const field of LEAF_PROSE_FIELDS) {
+    if (isNonEmptyString(input[field])) fields[field] = input[field] as string;
+  }
+  if (typeof input.agent_definition_loaded === 'boolean') {
+    fields.agent_definition_loaded = input.agent_definition_loaded;
+  }
+  if (Number.isInteger(input.findingsCount)
+    && (input.findingsCount as number) >= 0
+    && (input.findingsCount as number) <= MAX_LEAF_COUNT) {
+    fields.findingsCount = input.findingsCount as number;
+  }
+  for (const field of LEAF_PROSE_ARRAY_FIELDS) {
+    const values = stringArray(input[field]);
+    if (values !== null) fields[field] = values;
+  }
+  return fields;
+}

+        'priorMaxDurationMinutes', 'newMaxDurationMinutes', 'elapsedMinutesAtExtension',
+      ] as const;
+      // A budget with a missing or non-integer field cannot be replayed, so the
+      // row is refused rather than extended with a guessed ceiling.
+      if (!isObject(budget) || !budgetFields.every((field) => Number.isSafeInteger(budget[field]) && (budget[field] as number) >= 0)) {
+        return refusedUpcast(compatibility, 'run-extended-budget-missing');
+      }
+      data = {
+        priorTailDigest: context.prevEventHash,
+        sessionId: stringValue(input.sessionId, context.scope.runId),
+        generation: positiveInteger(input.generation, 1),
+        continuedFromRun: positiveInteger(input.continuedFromRun, 0),
+        extension: Object.fromEntries(budgetFields.map((field) => [field, budget[field] as number])) as JsonObject,
+      };
+      break;
+    }
     case 'deep_research.iteration_started':
       if (iteration === null) return refusedUpcast(compatibility, 'iteration-scope-missing');
       // The opening row names only its iteration and the executor that runs it,
@@ -657,6 +709,7 @@ export function upcastLegacyDeepResearchRecord(
             && question.trim().length > 0)
           ? { answeredQuestions: input.answeredQuestions }
           : {}),
> build · deepseek-v4.1-flash

repoRoot,
    artifactDir,
    stateLogPath,
    sessionId,
    iteration,
    label,
  });
  return dispatchExit;
}

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. EXECUTOR HANDLERS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function runClaude(context) {
  // Both loops validate the config dir before the prompt is read, so an unusable
  // config directory fails before any prompt or persona file is touched.
  const configDir = context.claudeConfigDir
    ? validateClaudeConfigDir(context.claudeConfigDir)
    : '';
  const promptBody = readPrompt(context);

  if (context.loopType === 'review') {
    const executor = {
      kind: 'cli-claude-code',
      model: context.model,
      configDir: configDir || null,
      reasoningEffort: context.reasoningEffort,
      serviceTier: null,
      sandboxMode: null,
      timeoutSeconds: context.timeoutSeconds,
      governor: null,
    };
    const args = [
      '-p',
      promptBody,
      '--model',
      executor.model,
      '--permission-mode',
      'acceptEdits',
      '--output-format',
      'text',
    ];
    if (executor.reasoningEffort) {
      args.push('--effort', executor.reasoningEffort);
    }

    // CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR rides through guardContext.env; the CLAUDE_ prefix is on the
    // cli-claude-code env allowlist so buildExecutorDispatchEnv admits it.
    const parentEnv = { ...process.env };
    if (configDir) {
      parentEnv.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR = configDir;
    }

    return runAuditedExecutorCommand({
      command: 'claude',
      args,
      cwd: context.repoRoot,
      timeoutSeconds: executor.timeoutSeconds,
      stateLogPath: context.stateLogPath,
      executor,
      iteration: context.iteration,
      guardContext: { env: parentEnv },
      receiptDir: context.receiptDir,
      dispatchId: context.dispatchId,
      sessionId: context.sessionId,
    });
  }

  const executor = {
    kind: 'cli-claude-code',
    model: context.model,
    configDir: configDir || null,
    reasoningEffort: context.reasoningEffort,
    serviceTier: context.serviceTier,
    sandboxMode: context.sandboxMode,
    timeoutSeconds: context.timeoutSeconds,
  };
  const args = [
    '-p',
    promptBody,
    '--model',
    executor.model,
    '--permission-mode',
    resolveClaudePermissionMode(executor.sandboxMode),
    '--output-format',
    'text',
  ];
  if (executor.reasoningEffort) {
    args.push('--effort', executor.reasoningEffort);
  }

  // CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR rides through guardContext.env; the CLAUDE_ prefix is on the
  // cli-claude-code env allowlist so buildExecutorDispatchEnv admits it.
  const parentEnv = { ...process.env };
  if (configDir) {
    parentEnv.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR = configDir;
  }

  return runAuditedExecutorCommand({
    command: 'claude',
    args,
    cwd: context.repoRoot,
    timeoutSeconds: executor.timeoutSeconds,
    stateLogPath: context.stateLogPath,
    executor,
    iteration: context.iteration,
    guardContext: { env: parentEnv },
    receiptDir: context.receiptDir,
    dispatchId: context.dispatchId,
    sessionId: context.sessionId,

+// An operator extension raises the iteration and time budgets of a run that is
+// already under way. The budget spent at extension time is recorded beside the
+// old and new ceilings, so a projection can tell a raised limit from the one the

.skilled/commands/deep/review.md:40:>    - `convergence_mode` (default `default` unless `--convergence-mode` is present)
.skilled/commands/deep/review.md:99:| `--convergence-mode` | `default` \| `off` \| `sliding-window` \| `divergent` | `default` keeps the anti-convergence `minIterations` floor. `off` disables convergence-driven STOP while max-iterations, pause and halt stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window, five snapshots by default. `divergent` routes an eligible convergence stop into a Council pivot rather than ending the run. |
.skilled/commands/deep/review.md:100:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and a run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap that defaults to half of `--max-iterations`), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open, and, once every node is closed and at least one was answered, forms a `frontier_drained` stop candidate that the legal-stop gates still decide, so the run can stop before its cap. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
.skilled/commands/deep/review.md:107:| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior review report as `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_dimensions_clean` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off` or use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`. |
.skilled/commands/deep/research.md:40:>    - `convergence_mode` (default `default` unless `--convergence-mode` is present)
.skilled/commands/deep/research.md:106:| `--convergence-mode` | `default` \| `off` \| `sliding-window` \| `divergent` | `default` keeps the anti-convergence `minIterations` floor. `off` disables convergence-driven STOP while max-iterations, pause and halt stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window, five snapshots by default, on the ordinary legal-stop path. `divergent` sends a `composite_converged` or `all_questions_answered` stop into a one-round three-seat Council pivot instead of ending the run, at proportionally higher cost. |
.skilled/commands/deep/research.md:107:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 frontier nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as new questions under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and a run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap that defaults to half of `--max-iterations`), steers Next Focus to the shallowest open node, and turns a convergence stop into another iteration while frontier nodes stay open. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 10 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
.skilled/commands/deep/research.md:114:| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior synthesis as `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_questions_answered` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations` or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`. |

          # Null derives the run-wide node cap at admission as half the iteration budget, so the frontier cannot claim every iteration; the depth-1 seed answers only to the root-children cap but counts toward this one, so growth past depth 1 needs maxIterations above twice the live node count.
          expansiveDepth.maxFrontierNodes: null
          antiConvergence.divergent: {}
          stopPolicy: "{stop_policy}"
          stuckThreshold: 2
          maxDurationMinutes: 120

          # Honor the parsed --no-resource-map flag from deep-review.md:71 (default true; false when flag is set).
          # The markdown command parses $ARGUMENTS and binds {resource_map_emit} before this step runs.
          resource_map.emit: "{resource_map_emit}"
          createdAt: "{ISO_8601_NOW}"
          status: "initialized"
          releaseReadinessState: "in-progress"
