return questions
    .map((question) => `- ${question.text} (iteration ${question.addedAtIteration || 0})`)
    .join('\n');
}

function deriveNextFocusFromContinuity({
  iterationFiles = [],
  iterationRecords = [],
  carriedForwardOpenQuestions = [],
  machineOpenQuestions = [],
  leafNextFocus = '',
  resolvedQuestions = [],
  terminalSentinel = DEFAULT_TERMINAL_NEXT_FOCUS,
} = {}) {
  const machineQuestions = toTextArray(machineOpenQuestions);
  if (machineQuestions.length === 0) {
    return terminalSentinel;
  }

  // The leaf's own recommendation is the freshest signal, but an answered recommendation
  // must not reopen settled work, and it never overrides the terminal sentinel.
  const leafFocus = normalizeText(leafNextFocus);
  const resolvedQuestionKeys = new Set(toTextArray(resolvedQuestions).map(normalizeKey));
  if (leafFocus && !resolvedQuestionKeys.has(normalizeKey(leafFocus))) {
    return truncateFocus(leafFocus);
  }

  const latestThreadQuestion = getLatestByRun(carriedForwardOpenQuestions);
  if (latestThreadQuestion?.text) {
    return latestThreadQuestion.text;
  }

  const latestIteration = getLatestByRun(iterationFiles);
  const latestFinding = Array.isArray(latestIteration?.findings)
    ? latestIteration.findings.map(normalizeText).filter(Boolean).at(-1)
    : null;
  if (latestFinding) {
    return `Follow up on: ${truncateFocus(latestFinding)}`;
  }

  const latestRecord = getLatestByRun(iterationRecords);
  const latestRecordFinding = Array.isArray(latestRecord?.findings)
    ? latestRecord.findings.map(textFromQuestion).filter(Boolean).at(-1)
    : null;
  if (latestRecordFinding) {
    return `Follow up on: ${truncateFocus(latestRecordFinding)}`;
  }

  return machineQuestions[0] || terminalSentinel;
}

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 5. EXPORTS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

module.exports = {
  buildCarriedForwardOpenQuestions,
  deriveNextFocusFromContinuity,
  formatCarriedForwardOpenQuestions,
};
---playbook claim---

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that deep-research picks its next focus from open machine questions and the latest leaf's Recommended Next Focus, and not from a status sentence or an answered question. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run deep-research-reduce-state.vitest -t "status sentence"`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run deep-research-reduce-state.vitest -t "Recommended Next Focus"`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run continuity-thread.vitest`
4. `rg -n "buildCarriedForwardOpenQuestions|deriveNextFocusFromContinuity" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-loop/continuity-thread.cjs`
5. `rg -n "resolveNextFocus|buildRegistry" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs`

### Expected

Commands 1 to 3 exit 0 with every selected case passing. The case that drops a status sentence listed under Questions Remaining passes. The case that reads the latest leaf Recommended Next Focus, and skips it when it is resolved, passes. Commands 4 and 5 print the carried-forward filter, the leaf read and the call sites that feed the registry.

### Evidence

- The vitest summary line and exit status for commands 1 to 3, with the selected case names.
- The `rg` line numbers for commands 4 and 5, read from the current source.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Commands 1 to 3 exit 0 with the named cases passing, and commands 4 and 5 show the filter and the leaf read.
- **Fail**: A status sentence or an answered question reaches Next Focus or the carried-forward list, or any named case fails.

### Failure Triage

- A named case is missing or renamed: the test file no longer pins the behavior, so restore the case before trusting the verdict.
- A status sentence reaches the carried-forward list: check the question-shape and answered-question filter in `buildCarriedForwardOpenQuestions`.
- Next Focus ignores an unresolved leaf recommendation while a machine question is open: check the leaf read in `deriveNextFocusFromContinuity`.

# v2.1.6.0, Research Runs Use the Configured Claude Config Dir

A research run on Claude ignored the config dir the operator set. It ran under whichever account the shell had. Research now honors the configured dir.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Research honors `--config-dir=PATH` on `cli-claude-code` runs.
- The dir is checked before the prompt is read. A dir outside the allowed roots is refused.
- Claude runs under the configured dir. It overrides an inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
- The audit record names the dir the run used.
- A run that names no dir behaves as before.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

New cases cover the flag, its validation and the audit record. All ten dispatch cases pass in a detached run. Inside a Claude session, two skip under the Claude ancestry guard.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Set `configDir` in the run's executor config to pin the account. An inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` still reaches a research run that names no dir. Stripping it would change every default run, so it stays as it is.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/002-research-claude-config-dir`

title: "deep-research v1.16.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Research"
description: "Deep research can now find its own sub-questions. An opt-in expansive depth mode admits, steers toward and closes them, and runs without the flag keep their output, apart from one prompt paragraph."
trigger_phrases:

  - "deep-research 1.16.0.0"
  - "expansive depth mode"
  - "frontier admission"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 1.16.0.0
---

# v1.16.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Research

Deep research stops once its starting questions are answered. This release adds an opt-in mode that lets a run grow its own questions.

- Leaves propose new sub-questions while the run works.
- Each proposal passes fixed checks before it joins the question set.

Runs without the flag keep their behavior.

- Nothing changes unless the flag is passed.
- The one difference is one paragraph in CLI lineage prompts.

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

A run that answers its starting questions narrowly looks finished. Nothing could add a question once the run began. So a broad topic could stop early.

- Narrow coverage looked the same as converged coverage.
- The new mode lets the run widen within fixed bounds.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

| Change | What It Means | Section |
|---|---|---|
| [Widen a run with one flag](#c-widen-a-run-with-one-flag) | One flag turns the mode on for a single run. | Turning It On |
| [Default runs keep their output](#c-default-runs-keep-their-output) | A run without the flag writes what it wrote before, apart from one prompt paragraph. | Turning It On |
| [See the frontier on the dashboard](#c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard) | An expansive run adds a frontier section to its dashboard. | Turning It On |
| [Open questions exist before the first pass](#c-open-questions-exist-before-the-first-pass) | The run has open sub-questions before iteration one. | Admitting New Questions |
| [Every proposal passes six fixed checks](#c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks) | Each proposal meets the same bounds in the same order. | Admitting New Questions |
| [Rejected proposals name their reason](#c-rejected-proposals-name-their-reason) | A pruned proposal records the check that stopped it. | Admitting New Questions |
| [Focus goes to the shallowest open node](#c-focus-goes-to-the-shallowest-open-node) | The next iteration works the least deep open question. | Steering and Stopping |
| [A run keeps going while nodes stay open](#c-a-run-keeps-going-while-nodes-stay-open) | A run that looks finished continues while a node is open. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Budget is shared across three depth bands](#c-budget-is-shared-across-three-depth-bands) | The iteration budget splits across depths. Unused budget rolls down. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Finished nodes stop counting as open](#c-finished-nodes-stop-counting-as-open) | A finished node resolves its question and records why it ended. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Lineages share the starting nodes](#c-lineages-share-the-starting-nodes) | A fan-out run deals its starting nodes across its lineages. | Fan-Out Runs |
| [The merge names a missing node](#c-the-merge-names-a-missing-node) | The merge warns when a starting node reached no lineage. | Fan-Out Runs |

&nbsp;

## Turning It On

Turning the mode on takes one flag. Without the flag, a run keeps its old behavior. Its dashboard gains no new section.

#### Widen a run with one flag <a id="c-widen-a-run-with-one-flag"></a>

- Pass `--depth-mode=expansive` to a research run.
- Leave the flag out to keep the old behavior.
- Passing `--depth-mode=default` also keeps the old behavior.
- `rg -n -- '--depth-mode' .skilled/commands/deep/research.md` finds the flag row.

#### Default runs keep their output <a id="c-default-runs-keep-their-output"></a>

- A default run writes the same registry, dashboard, focus and prompt pack as before.
- The stop config echo stays the same.
- The mode key appears only on expansive runs.
- CLI lineage prompts gain one paragraph in the agent persona.
- That paragraph has no effect on a default run.
- Its waiver awaits operator sign-off.

#### See the frontier on the dashboard <a id="c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard"></a>

- An expansive run adds a `## 6B. FRONTIER` section to its dashboard.
- The section shows frontier size and pruned nodes.
- It also shows revisits and budget spent at each depth.
- A default dashboard has no such section.

&nbsp;

## Admitting New Questions

Leaves propose sub-questions as rows. The run checks each one before it changes the question set.

#### Open questions exist before the first pass <a id="c-open-questions-exist-before-the-first-pass"></a>

- The run splits its topic into starting nodes before iteration one.
- The model running the loop writes those nodes.
- It works from the topic and its key questions.
- Every starting node passes the same checks.
- A repeated launch adds no second set of nodes.

#### Every proposal passes six fixed checks <a id="c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks"></a>

- The checks run in one fixed order.
- Relevance comes first, then dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle.
- Relevance needs evidence, a scope and a parent that exists.
- Dedupe refuses a question the run already holds.
- Depth stops at three levels.
- Fan-out caps each parent at six children.
- Fan-out also caps the total node count.
- A child cannot hold more budget than its parent.
- A cycle is refused.

#### Rejected proposals name their reason <a id="c-rejected-proposals-name-their-reason"></a>

- The first failing check decides the reason.
- A pruned proposal keeps its reason in the node store.
- A pruned proposal never reaches the research inbox.
- An admitted proposal adds one question with origin `frontier`.

&nbsp;

## Steering and Stopping

Once nodes exist, the run works the shallowest one. It stops only when no node stays open within budget.

#### Focus goes to the shallowest open node <a id="c-focus-goes-to-the-shallowest-open-node"></a>

- Next Focus picks the open node at the lowest depth.
- Ties go to a node with no covering finding.
- The oldest node wins any remaining tie.
- The focus line reads `Frontier node <id> (depth <n>, scope <scope>): <question>`.
- A visit to a node does not answer its question.
- A reworded focus still counts as a visit.
- It must keep the node's marker.

#### A run keeps going while nodes stay open <a id="c-a-run-keeps-going-while-nodes-stay-open"></a>

- A run that looks converged keeps going while a node is open within budget.
- The veto applies only when the expansive flag is on.
- The iteration cap still wins.
- Consecutive failures, stuck recovery and pause still win.
- The off setting and the max-iterations policy still win.
- A held run reports `frontierOpen` as its reason detail.

#### Budget is shared across three depth bands <a id="c-budget-is-shared-across-three-depth-bands"></a>

- The iteration budget splits 50, 35 and 15 percent across three depth bands.
- Unused budget rolls down to a deeper band.
- Unused budget never rolls up.
- A band that runs out closes its open nodes as exhausted for budget.

#### Finished nodes stop counting as open <a id="c-finished-nodes-stop-counting-as-open"></a>

- A node that finishes resolves its question and records its status.
- A finished node stops lowering question coverage and source diversity.
- A stuck run closes its active node as exhausted.
- Focus then moves on.
- A run whose nodes have all finished can reach a clean stop.

&nbsp;

## Fan-Out Runs

Fan-out runs share the starting nodes across their lineages. Then the merge checks the split.
> build · deepseek-v4.1-flash

name: sk-create-changelog

# create-changelog

`create-changelog` is the changelog-authoring workflow packet of the `sk-doc` family. It writes a global component changelog under `.skilled/changelog/{component}/v{VERSION}.md`. Packet-local (nested) changelogs under a spec packet's `changelog/` folder are deprecated, and this workflow no longer writes them (section 4).

This file is the executable contract: resolve the source, resolve the global target, calculate the version, generate, validate and write. `assets/changelog-template.md` owns what an entry says and how it reads. The `references/` set, routed by `references/README.md`, holds worked examples and edge cases.

---

## 1. WHEN TO USE

### Activation Triggers

Use this workflow to:

1. Create a global component changelog at `.skilled/changelog/{component}/v{VERSION}.md`.
2. Answer a request for a packet-local (nested) changelog: refuse it as deprecated and offer a global component entry instead.
3. Run or support `/create:changelog`.
4. Resolve a changed spec folder, component hint or recent git history into a changelog target.
5. Calculate the next four-part version for a global changelog.
6. Prepare GitHub release notes from a generated global changelog.

Keyword triggers: `create changelog`, `/create:changelog`, `changelog`, `changelog entry`, `release notes`, `global changelog`, `packet-local changelog`, `nested changelog`, `since the last version`, `vX.Y.Z.B`.

### When NOT to Use

1. A release plan with no file output.
2. A README (`create-readme`), agent, command, benchmark, feature catalog, manual testing playbook or skill. Each has its own `create-*` packet. A flowchart or any other diagram belongs to the `sk-design` hub (`/design:diagram`), which this hub does not register a packet for.
3. Auditing, scoring or optimizing an existing changelog (`create-quality-control`).
4. A source too ambiguous to resolve to a spec folder, a component hint or recent git history.
5. Branch, commit or PR work and release mechanics beyond `--release`, which `sk-git` owns. The tag and GitHub release run in the command YAMLs' release step (section 8), and only for the `skilled` release line.

---

## 2. SMART ROUTING

The `sk-doc` hub resolves a changelog-authoring request through `mode-registry.json`.
`hub-router.json` scores the request, the winning `workflowMode` is `sk-create-changelog`, and the hub loads this `SKILL.md` first.
A request to create a global component changelog, write release notes, calculate the next four-part version or resolve a changed source selects this packet.
A packet-local changelog request that lands here is refused as deprecated and offered a global entry.
The packet has one output mode, the global component entry.

`references/README.md` is the route map and the fallback load, and `assets/changelog-template.md` owns what an entry says and how it reads.

A request that matches no registered mode returns `UNKNOWN_FALLBACK`, never a silent default.

Read [`ROUTER.md`](./ROUTER.md) before selecting skill-local resources; open only the matched files.

---

## 3. REQUIRED INPUTS

| Input | Required | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `source_type` | Yes, resolved before writing | `spec_folder`, `component` or `git_history` |
| `spec_folder` | When `source_type = spec_folder` | The spec folder to summarize into a global component entry |
| `component_hint` | When `source_type = component` | A name matched against the discovered changelog folders |
| `version_bump` | Optional, default `auto` | `major`, `minor`, `patch`, `build` or `auto` |
| `--nested` | Refused | Packet-local changelogs are deprecated. The workflow stops and offers a global entry (section 4) |
| `--release` | Optional | After a `skilled` release entry is written, tags the version and publishes the GitHub release with the entry as its body (section 8). Any other component skips the release |

Global output lives under `.skilled/changelog/`. Versions read `v{MAJOR}.{MINOR}.{PATCH}.{BUILD}`, the first one is `v1.0.0.0` and dates use `YYYY-MM-DD`. Between 40 and 79 percent confidence in the source or target, proceed and record the assumptions. Below 40 percent, stop and ask with A/B/C options.

---

## 4. HOW IT WORKS: CHANGELOG OUTPUT MODES

### Global Component Changelog

A global entry is written to `.skilled/changelog/{component}/v{VERSION}.md`.
`{component}` is a lowercase kebab-case segment matching `^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$`, or `{hub}/{link}` when the folder is a hub, with each segment matching that pattern.
Reject an ambiguous hint rather than emit a guessed or underscore-bearing directory.
The `v{VERSION}.md` filename is an exact version contract and is never slug-normalized.

# Skilled Release Notes

> One entry per Skilled release, written for the operator who upgrades the framework.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This folder holds the framework's release notes, one entry per Skilled release. An entry's version is the git tag and GitHub release of the same number.

| Path | Holds |
|---|---|
| `v1+/`, `v2+/`, `v3+/` | Older entries, grouped by generation |
| `v4.0.0.0.md`, `v4.0.0.1.md`, `v4.0.0.2.md`, `v4.0.0.3.md` | Current generation entries at the top level |
| `v4.0.0.4.md` | Unreleased entry for the next release, with no tag or GitHub release yet |

An entry lives here when a GitHub release carries its version number. The entry whose tag does not exist yet is `v4.0.0.4.md`.

---

## 2. ADDING AN ENTRY

Run `/create:changelog skilled`. The workflow writes to this folder only when the operator names `skilled`.

Adding `--release` also tags `v<version>` and publishes the GitHub release, titled with the tag and the entry's editorial title.

House style: `v4.0.0.0.md` in this folder is the canonical exemplar that the changelog template points to.

---

## 3. VERSIONS

The scheme is `MAJOR.MINOR.SERIES.PATCH`. The version is also the release's git tag. Pass `--bump` explicitly for a release entry.

| `--bump` value | Bumps |
|---|---|
| `major` | MAJOR |
| `minor` | MINOR |
| `patch` | SERIES |
| `build` | PATCH |

---

Each skill keeps its own changelog in `.skilled/skills/<skill>/changelog/`, reachable as `.skilled/changelog/<skill>/`. system-spec-kit's own history, for example, is in `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/changelog/`.

| [`PUBLIC-RELEASE.md`](../../../PUBLIC-RELEASE.md) | Release process and version scheme |

---
title: "deep-review v1.13.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Review"
description: "Deep review can now split its target into sub-targets and work through them. An opt-in expansive depth mode admits, steers toward and closes them. Runs without the flag keep their output."
trigger_phrases:
  - "deep-review v1.13.0.0"

version: 1.13.0.0
---

# v1.13.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Review

Deep review can now split its target into sub-targets and work through them. This release adds an opt-in expansive depth mode for that.

- Reviews propose sub-targets while they work.
- Each proposal passes fixed checks before it joins the frontier.

Runs without the flag keep their behavior.

- Nothing changes unless the flag is passed.
- The one difference is one paragraph in CLI lineage prompts.

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

A review that checks its target in one pass can look finished too early. Nothing could split a sub-target out once the review began. So a large target could stop before each part got its own look.

- A narrow pass looked the same as a thorough one.
- The new mode lets the review widen within fixed bounds.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

| Change | What It Means | Section |
|---|---|---|
| [Widen a review with one flag](#c-widen-a-review-with-one-flag) | One flag turns the mode on for a single run. | Turning It On |
| [Default runs keep their output](#c-default-runs-keep-their-output) | A run without the flag writes what it wrote before, apart from one prompt paragraph. | Turning It On |
| [See the frontier on the dashboard](#c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard) | An expansive run adds a frontier section to its dashboard. | Turning It On |
| [Sub-targets exist before the first pass](#c-start-from-depth-one-sub-targets) | The review splits its target before iteration one. | Admitting Sub-Targets |
| [Every proposal passes six fixed checks](#c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks) | Each proposal meets the same bounds in the same order. | Admitting Sub-Targets |
| [Pruned proposals name their reason](#c-pruned-proposals-name-their-reason) | A pruned proposal records the check or the rule that stopped it. | Admitting Sub-Targets |
| [Each sub-target needs every dimension](#c-each-sub-target-needs-every-dimension) | A sub-target counts as answered only after all its dimensions. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Open sub-targets hold a clean stop](#c-open-sub-targets-hold-a-clean-stop) | A review that looks clean keeps going while a sub-target stays open. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Budget splits across three depth bands](#c-budget-splits-across-three-depth-bands) | The iteration budget splits 50, 35 and 15 percent across depths. | Steering and Stopping |
| [The pivot waits for a closed frontier](#c-the-pivot-waits-for-a-closed-frontier) | The divergent pivot runs only after the frontier closes. | Steering and Stopping |
| [Lineages share the sub-targets](#c-lineages-share-the-sub-targets) | A fan-out review deals its sub-targets across its lineages. | Fan-Out Runs |
| [The merge names a missing sub-target](#c-the-merge-names-a-missing-sub-target) | The merge warns when a dealt sub-target reached no lineage. | Fan-Out Runs |

&nbsp;

## Turning It On

Turning the mode on takes one flag. Without the flag, a review keeps its old behavior, and its dashboard gains no section.

#### Widen a review with one flag <a id="c-widen-a-review-with-one-flag"></a>

- Pass `--depth-mode=expansive` to a `/deep:review` run.
- Leave the flag out, or pass `--depth-mode=default`, to keep the old behavior.
- `rg -n -- '--depth-mode' .skilled/commands/deep/review.md` finds the flag row.

#### Default runs keep their output <a id="c-default-runs-keep-their-output"></a>

- A default run writes the same registry, dashboard, Next Focus, prompt pack and stop config as before.
- The mode key appears only on expansive runs.
- CLI lineage prompts gain one paragraph in the agent persona.
- That paragraph has no effect on a default run.
- Its waiver awaits operator sign-off.

#### See the frontier on the dashboard <a id="c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard"></a>

- An expansive review adds a `## 10B. FRONTIER` section to its dashboard.
- The section shows frontier size, pruned sub-targets, revisits and budget spent at each depth.
- A default dashboard has no such section.

&nbsp;

## Admitting Sub-Targets

A review splits its target into sub-targets before it starts. Each proposal passes fixed checks before it joins the frontier.

#### Sub-targets exist before the first pass <a id="c-start-from-depth-one-sub-targets"></a>

- The review splits its target into depth-one sub-targets before iteration one.
- Each sub-target gets a scope and a question.
- A repeated launch adds no second set of sub-targets.
- A review writes its sub-targets to its own store and never to an inbox.

#### Every proposal passes six fixed checks <a id="c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks"></a>

- The checks run in one fixed order.
- Relevance comes first, then dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle.
- Relevance needs evidence, a scope inside the review target and a parent that exists.
- Dedupe refuses a question the review already holds.
- Depth stops at three levels.
- Fan-out caps each parent at six children and caps the total count.
- A child cannot hold more budget than its parent.
- A cycle is refused.

#### Pruned proposals name their reason <a id="c-pruned-proposals-name-their-reason"></a>

- The first failing check decides the reason.
- A sub-target that arrives with a reason is settled at seed time.
- Two seed reasons exist: `merge-no-own-diff` for a merge with no diff of its own, and `generated-artifact` for a generated index rebuild.
- A pruned proposal keeps its reason in the store.
- Only an admitted proposal joins the frontier.

&nbsp;

## Steering and Stopping

Once sub-targets exist, the review works the active node's next uncovered dimension. It stops only when no sub-target stays open.

#### Each sub-target needs every dimension <a id="c-each-sub-target-needs-every-dimension"></a>

- A sub-target answers once every configured dimension has evidence behind it.
- The active sub-target's next uncovered dimension steers the next iteration.
- Next Focus picks the blocked-stop recovery first, then the pivot, then the active sub-target.
- The leaf's own focus comes after that, and the first uncovered dimension comes last.

#### Open sub-targets hold a clean stop <a id="c-open-sub-targets-hold-a-clean-stop"></a>

- While a sub-target stays open, a review stop candidate becomes `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen`.
- The hold covers the clean stop, which is a hard stop, as well as the composite stop.
- The iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, the off setting, the max-iterations policy and the review floor still decide first.
- Stuck recovery closes the active sub-target as `exhausted`, so a sub-target the review cannot fund stops holding the review open.

#### Budget splits across three depth bands <a id="c-budget-splits-across-three-depth-bands"></a>

- The iteration budget splits 50, 35 and 15 percent across depths one, two and three.
- Unused budget rolls down only after its band has no open sub-target, and it never rolls up.
- A band that spends its share closes its open sub-targets as `exhausted` with reason `budget`.

#### The pivot waits for a closed frontier <a id="c-the-pivot-waits-for-a-closed-frontier"></a>

- The divergent review pivot refuses to run while a sub-target stays open.
- It seeds one revisit direction for each sub-target the frontier closed as `exhausted` or `blocked`.
- A default run keeps its pivot unchanged.

&nbsp;

## Fan-Out Runs

A fan-out review deals its depth-one sub-targets across its lineages. The merge then checks the split.

#### Lineages share the sub-targets <a id="c-lineages-share-the-sub-targets"></a>

- Open depth-one sub-targets deal round-robin across the lineages, in node-id order.
- Each lineage writes its share to its own store before it spawns.
- A review lineage has no inbox.
- The orchestration summary records which lineage holds which sub-target.

#### The merge names a missing sub-target <a id="c-the-merge-names-a-missing-sub-target"></a>

- The merge checks that every dealt sub-target reached a lineage.
- A sub-target that no lineage reports gets a warning on stderr, named `frontier_partition_guard_failed`.
- The merged output carries `frontier_partition_guard` with the missing identifiers.
- The merge still completes.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

The default path was checked against a baseline. The baseline was captured before any review depth-mode code existed. The suites below ran on this branch.

#### Before and After <a id="c-before-and-after"></a>

| Check | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Count of `--depth-mode` in the review command reference | 0 matches at the baseline commit | 1 match, the flag row |
| Count of `"depthMode"` in the review config | 0 matches at the baseline commit | 1 match, the `default` value |
| Golden default-path suite | Not present at the baseline commit | 14 of 14 cases pass |

<details>
<summary><b>What it costs</b></summary>

- An expansive review runs one admission step on each iteration.
- The cost was not measured.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>Tests and scenarios</b></summary>

- Golden default path: 14 passed, 0 failed.
- Review admission helper: 18 passed, 0 failed.
- Shared admission table: 24 passed, 0 failed.
- Reducer: 32 passed, 0 failed.
- Stop decision: 92 passed, 0 failed.
- Stop decision YAML contract: 34 passed, 0 failed.
- Divergent pivot: 26 passed, 0 failed.
- Fan-out runner: 207 passed, 0 failed.
- Fan-out merge: 88 passed, 0 failed.
- Review playbook validator: PASS, with the seven new scenarios.
- Not yet run: the live pilot on a real review target. It runs on the integration tip after merge.

</details>

&nbsp;

## Upgrade Notes

No action is needed for existing reviews. Only a run that passes the flag changes.

#### Before You Start <a id="c-before-you-start"></a>

1. Keep your review commands as they are. They behave as before.

<details>
<summary><b>Turning It On</b></summary>

- Add `--depth-mode=expansive` to a review run to let it add sub-targets.
- Read the `## 10B. FRONTIER` section of the dashboard.

</details>

**Shared backend contracts (consolidation promotions).** Beyond the numbered entries above, the backend hosts a small set of generic plumbing the consumer modes import rather than duplicate: `lib/deep-loop/runtime-capabilities.cjs` (parameterized capability resolver, with byte-compatible per-skill shims), `lib/deep-loop/artifact-root.cjs` (canonical seam re-exporting `resolveArtifactRoot` from `system-spec-kit/shared/review-research-paths.cjs`), `lib/deep-loop/lifecycle-taxonomy.cjs` (terminal lifecycle enum: seven `stopReason` plus four `sessionOutcome` values) and `scripts/loop-lock.cjs` (CLI adapter over `loop-lock.ts`). These contracts register no MCP tools and carry no public workflow routing; resource-map emission stays in the workflow shared-synthesis layer, not in this runtime.

## 2. EXECUTOR

These entries cover executor configuration, provenance audit, recursion guards, and model fallback decisions for deep-loop dispatch.

### Executor config

#### Description

Parses and normalizes per-iteration executor configuration for native and CLI-backed deep-loop dispatch.

#### How It Works

Schema, parsing, defaults, supported flags, sandbox and permission-mode normalization.

#### Source Files

See [`executor/executor-config.md`](../feature-catalog/executor/executor-config.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Executor audit

#### Description

Records executor provenance and guards recursive external-CLI dispatch inside iteration state logs.

#### How It Works

Recursion guard, executor audit record writing, dispatch-failure emission, and audited command spawning.

#### Source Files

See [`executor/executor-audit.md`](../feature-catalog/executor/executor-audit.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Fallback router

#### Description

Chooses whether a failed model should fall back to a configured target or fail fast.

#### How It Works

Model registry lookup, fallback target selection, disabled fallback, and fail-fast reasons.

#### Source Files

See [`executor/fallback-router.md`](../feature-catalog/executor/fallback-router.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Fallback-router typed reroute

#### Description

Adds typed fallback-route metadata, route trace fields, and startup graph validation for executor fallback routing.

#### How It Works

Fallback routes can declare success and failure targets, every decision can carry `routeGroupId` and `hopIndex`, and `validateFallbackGraph()` checks missing targets, cycles, scope widening, and hop limits before dispatch.

#### Source Files

See [`executor/fallback-router-typed-reroute.md`](../feature-catalog/executor/fallback-router-typed-reroute.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Research Claude config dir

#### Description

A deep-research iteration on cli-claude-code runs Claude under the configured config dir, and the executor audit records that dir.

#### How It Works

`--claude-config-dir` is optional for research and required for review on `cli-claude-code`. Every other executor refuses the flag.

#### Source Files

See [`executor/research-claude-config-dir.md`](../feature-catalog/executor/research-claude-config-dir.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Landed iteration is not redispatched

#### Description

A research or review iteration that already landed is not dispatched a second time when its verify check fails with executor_missing, findings_not_enumerated or ledger_backing_missing.

#### How It Works

The `redispatch_once` filter in both command YAMLs skips these reasons, and no iteration is dispatched more than once.

#### Source Files

See [`executor/landed-iteration-no-redispatch.md`](../feature-catalog/executor/landed-iteration-no-redispatch.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

## 3. PROMPT RENDERING

# Deep Start Review Loop

Thin router for the deep-review loop. This command verifies the orchestrating agent, resolves setup and execution mode, loads the presentation contract, then executes the owned workflow YAML.

### MANDATORY INPUT GATE

- **DO NOT** dispatch any agent from this document.
- **ALL** agent dispatching is handled by the YAML workflow steps.
- **MARKDOWN OWNS SETUP**: resolve setup inputs here first, then hand off to YAML.
- **YAML START CONDITION**: do not load YAML until ALL required inputs are bound:
  - `review_target`, `review_target_type`, `review_dimensions`, `spec_folder`, `execution_mode`, `maxIterations`, `convergenceThreshold`, `convergence_mode`, `depth_mode`, `stop_policy`, `lineage_mode`
  - `dry_run` (default false unless `--dry-run` is present)

## 1. ROUTER CONTRACT

Do not dispatch agents from this Markdown file. Agent dispatch, workflow steps, and artifact-writing behavior are owned by the workflow YAML assets.

Load the presentation contract before showing startup questions, dashboards, checkpoints, success output, failure output, examples, or next-step prompts.


> **EXECUTION PROTOCOL -- READ FIRST**
>
> This command runs a structured YAML workflow. Do NOT dispatch agents from this document.
>
> **YOUR FIRST ACTION (HARD-BLOCK gate — do not skip):**
> 1. Run the Unified Setup Phase (BLOCKED gate) through the presentation contract and resolve:
>    - `review_target`
>    - `review_target_type`
>    - `review_dimensions`
>    - `spec_folder`
>    - `execution_mode`
>    - `maxIterations`
>    - `convergenceThreshold`
>    - `convergence_mode` (default `default` unless `--convergence-mode` is present)
>    - `depth_mode` (default `default` unless `--depth-mode=expansive` is present)
>    - `stop_policy` (default `convergence` unless `--stop-policy=max-iterations` is present)
>    - `lineage_mode` (default `auto` unless `--lineage-mode` or `--restart` is present)
>    - `dry_run` (default false unless `--dry-run` is present)
> 2. Load the workflow YAML from `assets/` only after all setup values are resolved: `deep-review.yaml`. The mode binds `execution_mode` to `auto` or `confirm`.
> 3. Execute the YAML workflow step by step using those resolved values
>
> This command is **general-agent based** — it orchestrates the deep-review loop. The Unified Setup Phase (the BLOCKED gate) is a HARD BLOCK; it may not be skipped.

This command is **general-agent based**. Do not use the Task tool to spawn the general agent; the default primary agent is the command orchestrator when it has the tools listed below. Keep raw agent handles out of this command body because OpenCode parses them as delegation requests.

In `:auto` mode, an explicit `--restart` or `--lineage-mode=restart` flag (invocation form `--restart|--lineage-mode=restart`) is operator authorization to archive the resolved review packet before fan-out or phase init. Do not ask for a second confirmation; preserve rollback by moving the timestamped archive directory back to `review/` if needed.

### AUTONOMOUS EXECUTION DIRECTIVE (:auto)

> Read this first. It is an imperative to you, the executor — not background information.

You were invoked with the `:auto` suffix and a bound spec_folder and review target in your message. Setup is already resolved.

- DO NOT emit the consolidated setup question (target/mode, review target, Spec Folder A/B/C/D, execution mode, max iterations, executor). The bound spec_folder is your write authority; do not ask which spec folder should own the writes.

A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 7 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
| `--lineage-timeout-hours` | `N` | See Lineage Timeout Flag below. |
| `--stop-policy` | `convergence` \| `max-iterations` | See Stop Policy Flag below. |
| `--no-resource-map` | no value | Suppresses the resource-map write; the review report still emits. |
| `--dry-run` | no value | See Dry-Run Flag below. |

Which of those a given kind supports is in the presentation asset. |
| `--executors` | `JSON` | Declares the executor matrix in one value; one expanded lineage selects `config.executor`, while two or more select `config.fanout`. |
| `--concurrency` | `N` | Caps concurrent lineages in the fan-out pool. Default `2`. |

Fan-out policy: zero or one `--executor` runs the single-executor path, and so does an
`--executors` matrix that expands to exactly one lineage. Its `iterations` value binds to
`config.maxIterations`, and its executor settings populate `config.executor`. Two or more

title: "Custom review dimensions"

# Custom review dimensions

## 1. OVERVIEW

A review normally audits four canonical dimensions: correctness, security, traceability and maintainability. A setup can name more. A configured name outside the canonical four is a custom dimension. It is tracked and covered like the others, ordered after the canonical four, and judged by its own name. It is not required for severity coverage.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Setup And Ordering

`.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` accepts custom names at setup, normalizes them, and orders them after the canonical four in the risk order. The review registry seeds the four canonical keys plus the custom keys.

### Coverage Tracking

`runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` derives each finding's dimension by matching it against the configured dimension universe, so a custom name that appears in the finding's focus is kept as its own dimension. When nothing matches, the derivation falls back to the iteration's first addressed dimension and then to `correctness`. `buildDimensionCoverage` builds coverage from the iteration records and the strategy dimensions.

### Review Contract And Agent

`deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` carries a `customDimensionRule`: priority after the canonical four, configured order, judged by its own name under the shared P0/P1/P2 severity doctrine, and `requiredForSeverityCoverage: false`. The review agent definition allows a configured custom dimension under the same rubric rule.

### Stop Behavior

A covered custom dimension counts toward the all-dimensions-clean stop, so a review with every configured dimension covered and clean can reach that stop.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | Accepts, normalizes and orders custom names, and seeds the registry keys. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Derives each finding's dimension against the configured universe and builds dimension coverage. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` | Contract | The `customDimensionRule` block. |
| `.skilled/agents/deep-review.md` | Agent contract | Allows a configured custom dimension under the custom rubric rule. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-depth-convergence.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The custom dimension stop-path case. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-review-state-reducer.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Dimension coverage with custom names. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/initialization-and-state-setup/custom-dimension-setup-and-universe.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-074: a configured custom dimension joins the review dimension set. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/review-depth-v2-rollout/stop-gate-custom-dimension-coverage.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-075: a covered custom dimension reaches the all-dimensions-clean stop. |

[0m$ [0mcat .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md | sed -n '30,60p'; echo '====PLAYBOOK===='; sed -n '1,90p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/fanout/fanout-frontier-partition-round-robin.md
### Stop Behavior

A review stop candidate becomes another iteration while nodes stay open. The `frontierOpen` veto applies to the review stop candidates, including `all_dimensions_clean` and the composite stop, after the minIterations floor. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide.

### Pivot

The divergent review pivot runs only once the frontier is terminal. It seeds from terminal unanswered nodes, and an accepted candidate becomes the next review direction through `restoredFocus`. The pivot writes no frontier node.

### Fan-Out

Fan-out lineages receive a share of the seeded nodes. `runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` returns the merged review frontier and runs the review fan-in guard over the recorded partition.

---

title: "DRV-082 -- Review fan-out deals seeded frontier nodes round-robin"
description: "This scenario validates that a review fan-out run under the expansive mode deals its seeded depth-one nodes round-robin across its lineages, writes each lineage store without an inbox and reports a missing node through the merge guard, for `DRV-082`."
version: 1.13.2.0

# DRV-082 -- Review fan-out deals seeded frontier nodes round-robin

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-082`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates how a review fan-out run splits its depth-one nodes across lineages and checks the split at merge time for `DRV-082`. It focuses on the round-robin deal in node-id order, on each lineage's node store, which is written before the lineage spawns and carries no inbox, and on the merge guard that names a dealt node no lineage reports.

### Why This Matters

A fan-out review that drops a sub-target without saying so looks complete. The partition makes each node belong to one lineage, and the guard makes a lost node visible at merge time instead of letting the merged frontier look finished.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the commands below and confirm that the deal is round-robin, that each lineage store is written before spawn, that a default run deals nothing and that the merge guard names a missing node.

- Objective: Confirm that depth-one nodes are dealt round-robin in node-id order across lineages, that each lineage store is written with no inbox, that a default lineage prompt gains no depth-mode bytes and that the merge guard fails and names a dealt node that no lineage reports.
- Real user request: Split the review across lineages and show me that no sub-target is dropped.
- Prompt: `Check that a review fan-out run under expansive depth mode deals its depth-one nodes round-robin across its lineages, writes each lineage store without an inbox and fails the merge guard when a dealt node reaches no lineage. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the review fan-out cases by file stem, run the review merge cases by describe name, then read the partition helper, the store writer and the guard line in the fan-out and merge scripts.
- Expected signals: Seven nodes deal to the three lineages as left `F-1-001`, `F-1-004`, `F-1-007`, center `F-1-002`, `F-1-005` and right `F-1-003`, `F-1-006`. The lineage store lands at the lineage directory root with no `inbox.jsonl` file. The default lineage prompt gains no depth-mode bytes. The merge guard passes when every dealt node is reported and fails with the missing identifier when one is not. The merge output carries `frontier_partition_guard`.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say every seeded sub-target reached a lineage, or the merge names the one that did not.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the fan-out cases, the merge cases and the guard line pass or print; FAIL if the deal is not round-robin, a lineage store is missing before spawn, an inbox appears for a review lineage, or a missing node passes the guard.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that a review fan-out run under expansive depth mode deals its depth-one nodes round-robin across its lineages, writes each lineage store without an inbox and fails the merge guard when a dealt node reaches no lineage. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-run.vitest -t "deals seven review frontier nodes|writes a review lineage store without an inbox|binds a review frontier scope|adds no depth-mode bytes to a default review lineage prompt" --reporter=verbose`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run fanout-merge.vitest -t "review frontier merge" --reporter=verbose`
3. `rg -n "partitionFrontierNodes|writeLineageFrontierStore|frontier_scope" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs`
4. `rg -n -F "frontier_partition_guard_failed" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  4 passed` (the rest of the file skipped). The round-robin case asserts the deal above, the store case asserts a `frontier-nodes.jsonl` file at the lineage directory root with no `inbox.jsonl`, the scope case asserts the `frontier_scope` binding beside `review_focus`, and the default case asserts no depth-mode bytes in the lineage prompt. Command 2 prints `Tests  5 passed` (the rest of the file skipped). Its cases pass the guard when every dealt node is reported, fail it with `missing: ['F-1-004']` when one is not, merge each lineage frontier with its label, give every merged node a lineage-qualified id when lineages number their children alike and add no frontier key when no review lineage has one. Command 3 prints the partition helper, the store writer, the `frontier_scope` prompt bindings and the store call, which passes `inbox: false` for a review lineage. Command 4 prints one line, which writes the `frontier_partition_guard_failed` warning with the missing identifiers.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary lines from commands 1 and 2, with each exit status.
- The `rg` lines from commands 3 and 4, read from the current scripts.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Commands 1 and 2 exit 0 with the expected counts, and commands 3 and 4 print their lines.
- **Fail**: The deal is not round-robin in node-id order, a review lineage gets an inbox, a missing node passes the guard, or command 4 prints nothing.

### Failure Triage

1. The deal differs from the round-robin order: check the partition helper, which must take the open or active depth-one nodes, sort them by node id and then deal them across the labels in turn.
2. A lineage store lands in the wrong place or appears with an inbox: the lineage store belongs at `lineages/<label>/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, and a review lineage must not receive an inbox.
3. The merge guard stays silent on a missing node: check that the guard reads the recorded partition from the orchestration summary, and that the merged output carries `frontier_partition_guard`.

      lineage: entry.lineage,
      findingId: entry.findingId,
      unknownSeverity: entry.severity,
      message: `Finding "${entry.findingId}" from lineage "${entry.lineage}" carries severity "${entry.severity}", which is outside the review severity scale (P0|P1|P2). It is unranked, sorts below every P2, and cannot raise the merged verdict; collapse it to P2 where the finding is rated.`,
    };
    schemaWarnings.push(warning);
    process.stderr.write(JSON.stringify(warning) + '\n');
  }

  return {
    mergedFrom: lineageData.map(({ label }) => label).sort(),
    lineageExecutors: buildLineageExecutors(lineageData),
    mergedVerdict,
    failedRequiredGateIds,
    openFindings: mergedFindings,
    resolvedFindings: mergedResolvedFindings,
    findingsBySeverity: { P0: activeP0, P1: activeP1, P2: activeP2 },
    openFindingsCount: mergedFindings.length,
    resolvedFindingsCount: mergedResolvedFindings.length,
    activeP0,
    activeP1,
    activeP2,
    searchDebt: mergedSearchDebt,
    ...mergeReviewCoverageSurfaces(lineageData),
    ...(schemaWarnings.length > 0 ? { schema_mismatch: schemaWarnings } : {}),
    ...(hasFrontier ? { frontier: mergeResearchFrontier(lineageData, options.frontierPartition) } : {}),
  };
}

## 1. WHEN TO USE

### When to Use This Skill

Use this skill when:
- Code quality audit requiring multiple rounds across different review dimensions
- Spec folder validation requiring cross-reference checks between docs and implementation
- Release readiness check before shipping a feature or component
- Finding misalignments between spec documents and actual code
- Verifying cross-references across documentation, agents, commands, and code
- Iterative review where each dimension's findings inform subsequent dimensions
- Unattended or overnight audit sessions

### When NOT to Use

- Simple single-pass code review (use `sk-code`'s code-review mode instead)
- Known issues that just need fixing (go directly to implementation)
- Implementation tasks (use `sk-code` or `/speckit:implement`)
- Quick one-file checks (use direct Grep/Read)
- Fewer than 2 review dimensions needed (single-pass suffices)

### FORBIDDEN INVOCATION PATTERNS

This skill is invoked EXCLUSIVELY through the `/deep:review` command. The command's YAML workflow owns state, dispatch, and convergence.

---research---

### Activation Triggers

Use this skill when:
- Deep investigation requiring multiple rounds of discovery
- Topic spans 3+ technical domains or sources
- Initial findings need progressive refinement
- Overnight or unattended research sessions
- Research where prior findings inform subsequent queries

Keyword triggers:

- `autoresearch`
- `deep research`
- `autonomous research`
- `research loop`
- `iterative research`
- `multi-round research`
- `deep investigation`
- `comprehensive research`

### Use Cases

Use deep-research for multi-round technical investigation, source triangulation, repeated exploration with fresh context, and research sessions where prior findings should shape the next focus.

### When NOT to Use

// A review leaf names a dimension with the short prefix it uses locally. The graph keeps
// one id shape, so a prefixed name folds to that shape before a node or edge is stored.
function canonicalReviewDimensionId(id) {
  if (typeof id !== 'string') return id;
  const match = /^(?:dimension:|dim:|dim-)(.+)$/i.exec(id.trim());
  if (!match) return id;
  return `dimension:${match[1].toLowerCase()}`;
}

// A review leaf names a finding node with its own prefix and case around the finding
// id. The graph keeps one finding node per finding, so `finding-R1-P1-001`,
// `finding-r1-p1-001`, `FIND-R1-P1-001`, `node:finding:R1-P1-001` and the bare id all
// fold onto the node the finding is stored under. `knownFindings` maps each lowercased
// finding id of this iteration and of the stored graph to that node id. The raw id is
// tried before its prefix is stripped, so an id that itself starts with `finding-`
// resolves the same way in every iteration that names it.
function resolveReviewFindingId(id, knownFindings) {
  if (typeof id !== 'string') return null;
  const trimmed = id.trim();
  const direct = knownFindings.get(trimmed.toLowerCase());
  if (direct) return direct;
  const match = /^(?:node:)?(?:finding|find)[:_-](.+)$/i.exec(trimmed);
  if (!match) return null;
  return knownFindings.get(match[1].toLowerCase()) ?? `finding:${match[1]}`;
}

// A file:line ref names a place a finding can be checked; a bare path does not. The
// shape matches the one the stop decision counts as file:line evidence, and a
// trailing column or range is dropped.
function parseFindingRef(ref) {
  if (typeof ref !== 'string') return null;
  const match = /^(.+?):(\d+)(?:[:-].*)?$/.exec(ref.trim());
  if (!match) return null;
  const filePath = match[1].replace(/^\.\//, '').trim();
  return filePath ? { filePath, line: Number(match[2]) } : null;
}

// An optional source that does not exist reads as no rows; any other read failure
// is an input error, so a required log is never silently treated as empty.
function readJsonlRows(filePath, label, required) {
  let raw;
  }
  if (typeof stateLog === 'string') {
    let record = null;
    for (const row of readJsonlRows(stateLog, 'state log', true)) {
      if (row && row.type === 'iteration' && Number(row.iteration) === iteration) record = row;
    }
    for (const finding of record && Array.isArray(record.findingDetails) ? record.findingDetails : []) add(finding);
  }
  return [...byId.values()];
}

// Finding ids are unique only per source, so an id an earlier iteration stored for a
// different finding (another file and another title) gets a node keyed on the id and
// this iteration, the way the reducer keeps two such findings apart.
function deriveReviewFindingEvidence(findings, iteration, storedNode = () => null) {
  const nodes = [];
  const edges = [];
  for (const finding of findings) {
    if (!finding || typeof finding.id !== 'string' || !finding.id.trim()) continue;
    const rawId = finding.id.trim();
    const ref = parseFindingRef(finding.file);
    const title = typeof finding.title === 'string' && finding.title ? finding.title : rawId;
    const stored = storedNode(`finding:${rawId}`);
    const storedMeta = stored && stored.metadata && typeof stored.metadata === 'object' ? stored.metadata : {};
    const distinct = stored
      && typeof storedMeta.file === 'string'
      && ref !== null
      && storedMeta.file !== ref.filePath
      && stored.name !== title;
    const findingId = distinct ? `finding:${rawId}#${iteration}` : `finding:${rawId}`;
    nodes.push({
      id: findingId,
      kind: 'FINDING',
      name: title,
      iteration,
      metadata: { finding_id: rawId, ...(ref ? { file: ref.filePath, line: ref.line } : {}) },
    });
    if (!ref) continue;
    const fileId = `file:${ref.filePath}`;
    nodes.push({ id: fileId, kind: 'FILE', name: ref.filePath, iteration });
    edges.push({
      id: `evidence:${findingId.slice('finding:'.length)}:${fileId}`,
      relation: 'EVIDENCE_FOR',
      sourceId: findingId,
      targetId: fileId,
      metadata: { derivedFrom: 'findingDetails', line: ref.line },
    });
  }
  return { nodes, edges };
}

// A research SOURCE node's quality class comes from where the source lives, never
// from a value the leaf sends, so source diversity rises only when a finding cites
// sources of genuinely different kinds. `classes` is the frozen vocabulary the
// coverage-graph query module exports, so no class name is repeated here.
const DOC_HOST_PREFIXES = ['docs.', 'developer.', 'learn.', 'devdocs.'];
const DOC_HOSTS = new Set(['developer.mozilla.org', 'nodejs.org', 'typescriptlang.org', 'www.typescriptlang.org', 'vitest.dev']);
const DOC_EXTENSIONS = new Set(['.md', '.mdx', '.txt', '.rst']);
const TEST_FILE_PATTERN = /\.(?:test|spec|vitest)\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/i;
const TEST_DIR_NAMES = new Set(['test', 'tests', '__tests__']);

      : parseJsonValue(args.edges, [], 'edges');
    if (!Array.isArray(rawNodes) || !Array.isArray(rawEdges)) throw inputError('nodes and edges must be JSON arrays');
    const seedOptions = parseSeedOptions(args, rawNodes);
    let derived = { nodes: [], edges: [] };
    const knownFindings = new Map();
    const canonicalFindings = args.stateLog !== undefined || args.deltaDir !== undefined;
    if (canonicalFindings || args.iteration !== undefined) {
      if (loopType !== 'review') throw inputError('--state-log, --delta-dir and --iteration are supported only for loop type "review"');
      if (args.stateLog !== undefined && typeof args.stateLog !== 'string') throw inputError('--state-log requires a path');
      if (args.deltaDir !== undefined && typeof args.deltaDir !== 'string') throw inputError('--delta-dir requires a path');
      if (!canonicalFindings) throw inputError('--iteration needs --state-log or --delta-dir');
      const iteration = Number(args.iteration);
      if (!Number.isInteger(iteration) || iteration < 1) throw inputError('--iteration must be a positive integer');
      const findings = readReviewIterationFindings({ stateLog: args.stateLog, deltaDir: args.deltaDir, iteration });
      const findingNs = { specFolder, loopType, sessionId };
      derived = deriveReviewFindingEvidence(findings, iteration, (id) => db.getNode(findingNs, id));
      for (const stored of db.getNodesByKind(findingNs, 'FINDING')) {
        const storedMeta = stored.metadata && typeof stored.metadata === 'object' ? stored.metadata : {};
        const findingKey = typeof storedMeta.finding_id === 'string'
          ? storedMeta.finding_id
          : stored.id.replace(/^finding:/, '');
        if (!knownFindings.has(findingKey.toLowerCase())) knownFindings.set(findingKey.toLowerCase(), stored.id);
      }
      for (const node of derived.nodes) {
        if (node.kind !== 'FINDING') continue;
        const key = node.metadata.finding_id.toLowerCase();
        if (!knownFindings.has(key)) knownFindings.set(key, node.id);
      }
    }
    if (rawNodes.length === 0 && rawEdges.length === 0 && derived.nodes.length === 0) {
      if (!seedOptions) throw inputError('At least one node or edge must be provided');
    }

    const validationErrors = [];
    const rejectedSelfLoops = [];
    const nodes = [];
    const edges = [];
    // A leaf's own dimension or finding id shape must not add a second node for one
    // the graph already holds. This map carries an old id to the canonical one so an
    // edge that still names the old id lands on the same node.
    const reviewNodeIds = new Map();
    const batchRawKinds = new Map();

    for (const n of rawNodes) {
      const kind = String(n.kind || n.nodeKind || n.type || '').toUpperCase();
      if (!n.id || typeof n.id !== 'string') {
        validationErrors.push('Node missing required id');
        continue;
      }
      const validKinds = db.VALID_KINDS[loopType];
      if (!validKinds.includes(kind)) {
        validationErrors.push(`Invalid node kind "${kind}" for loop type "${loopType}". Valid: ${validKinds.join(', ')}`);
        continue;
      }
      let nodeId = n.id;
      if (loopType === 'review' && kind === 'DIMENSION') {
        const canonicalId = canonicalReviewDimensionId(n.id);
        if (canonicalId !== n.id) reviewNodeIds.set(n.id, canonicalId);
        nodeId = canonicalId;
      }
      if (canonicalFindings && kind === 'FINDING') {
        const canonicalId = resolveReviewFindingId(n.id, knownFindings) ?? n.id;
        if (canonicalId !== n.id) reviewNodeIds.set(n.id, canonicalId);
        nodeId = canonicalId;
      }
      if (loopType === 'review') batchRawKinds.set(n.id, kind);
      const name = n.name || n.label || n.id;
      // The derived class overwrites any leaf-supplied quality_class on a research
      // SOURCE node; every other node keeps its metadata as sent.
      const metadata = loopType === 'research' && kind === 'SOURCE'
        ? {
          ...(n.metadata && typeof n.metadata === 'object' ? n.metadata : {}),
          quality_class: deriveSourceQualityClass(n, SOURCE_QUALITY_CLASS),
        }
        : n.metadata;
      const baseNode = {
        id: nodeId,
        specFolder,
        sessionId,
        kind,
        name,
        metadata,
      };
      nodes.push({
        ...baseNode,
        loopType,
        contentHash: n.contentHash,
        iteration: n.iteration,
        seedSource: seedOptions?.seedSource,
        seedConfidence: seedOptions?.seedConfidence,
      });
    }

    const ns = { specFolder, loopType, sessionId };
    const batchNodeIds = new Set(nodes.map((node) => node.id));
    // A derived node never overwrites one the leaf sent or an earlier iteration stored,
    // so the leaf's own name and metadata stay; only a missing node is declared.
    for (const node of derived.nodes) {
      if (batchNodeIds.has(node.id) || db.getNode(ns, node.id)) continue;
      batchNodeIds.add(node.id);
      nodes.push({ ...node, specFolder, sessionId, loopType });
> build · deepseek-v4.1-flash

# system-deep-loop

> Run iterative research or an iterative code review as a loop that keeps its state on disk and stops on a convergence signal.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

### At a Glance

| Aspect | What it delivers |
|---|---|
| **Use it for** | Multi-round research or a multi-pass review. Each round builds on what the round before it found |
| **Invoke with** | `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint. A `/deep:*` command such as `/deep:research` or `/deep:review` also reaches it |
| **Works on** | A research topic with web or code sources. A review target such as a spec folder or a file set |
| **Produces** | The selected mode's artifacts and convergence state under the target spec folder, such as `research/research.md` or `review/review-report.md` |

### What It Does

`system-deep-loop` is one routing hub for two active loops. A request for iterative investigation goes to the `deep-research/` packet, which writes `research/research.md` under the target spec folder. A request for an iterative audit or a release-readiness verdict goes to `deep-review/`, which writes `review/review-report.md` and returns a verdict. Each packet owns its convergence math, state files, artifacts and tool guards. The shared `runtime/` beneath both modes holds executor config, prompt-pack rendering, atomic state and the coverage graph.

One-shot retrieval belongs to `@context`. Implementation planning belongs to `/speckit:plan`. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`.

### Why This Skill Exists

A deep-loop request can mean outward investigation or inward audit. The two loops share the same `runtime/` and the same append-only state path. One hub gives the family a single entry point, so the same request reaches the loop that owns it without a guess. The two packets still keep their own contracts, so one entry does not flatten the modes into one.

---

## 2. QUICK START

**Step 1: Invoke it.** Run `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint such as `research: <request>`. You can also call `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. Every surface reaches the same packet.

**Step 2: Check where a request routes.**

```bash
node .skilled/bin/compiled-route.cjs --hub system-deep-loop --prompt "deep review of the checklist changes"
```

Expected output: one JSON decision with `"action":"route"` and a target where `"workflowMode":"review"`. Exit code 0. Swap the prompt to `deep research on the routing drift` to see the research route.

**Step 3: Run the mode.** Pass the request to `/deep:review:auto "skill deep-review"`. The command workflow initializes `{spec_folder}/review/`, dispatches one review iteration per pass and synthesizes the report. The run produces `review-report.md` with a verdict, a findings registry, remediation workstreams and an audit appendix, plus the iteration files under `{spec_folder}/review/iterations/`.

---

## 3. HOW IT WORKS

Routing runs in two stages. Stage one picks the mode: the hub scores the request against the vocabulary classes in `hub-router.json`. The winning `workflowMode` selects one entry in `mode-registry.json`. Stage two picks the leaves: `ROUTER.md` maps the request's intent to packet-qualified reference files. Each path resolves to a canonical pair in `leaf-manifest.json`. The packet then runs its loop. It initializes state, dispatches one fresh agent per iteration, checks convergence and synthesizes its artifact. The hub only routes, so per-mode logic stays in the packet.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
  Request["Deep-loop request"] --> Hub["Hub scores the request"]
  Hub -->|research| Research["deep-research/ packet"]
  Hub -->|review| Review["deep-review/ packet"]
  Hub -->|no mode| Defer["defer: ask for the mode"]
  Research --> Runtime["runtime/"]
  Review --> Runtime
  Runtime --> Output["Artifacts and convergence state"]
```

### The Three-Tier Discriminator

Every registry entry carries three keys. `workflowMode` is the public mode key. Its values are `research` and `review`. `runtimeLoopType` is the convergence key that `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` consumes. The router reads it from the registry rather than inferring it from `workflowMode`. `backendKind` names the backend that runs the mode, `runtime-loop-type` for both active modes. A custom mode would declare an explicit `null` and name its own backend path.

### One Advisor Identity

Each packet carries no `graph-metadata.json`. The hub root carries the only one, so the advisor sees one skill identity no matter which surface reaches a mode.

---
> build · deepseek-v4.1-flash
[0m$ [0msed -n '80,240p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md
---

## 4. INTEGRATION & NAVIGATION

### When To Use This Skill

Reach for the hub when a request needs more than one round. Use the research mode for a topic that spans several sources, where each round should shape the next query. Use the review mode for a multi-pass audit of a spec folder or a file set. Use it for a release-readiness verdict. Both modes fit an unattended run, because each keeps its state on disk and checks convergence after every pass.

Use `@context` for a one-shot lookup and `/speckit:plan` for implementation planning. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`. A planning council is a separate agent, reached through `/speckit:ai-council`. It is not a mode of this hub.

### Related Skills

| Skill | Relationship |
|---|---|
| `system-spec-kit` | Owns the spec packets, validation and memory continuity that both modes read and write. |
| `sk-code` | Its code-review mode handles a single-pass review. The `deep-review/` packet runs the multi-pass loop. |

---

## 5. TROUBLESHOOTING

| What you see | Why | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| The compiled route returns `"action":"defer"` | The prompt names no mode, because `routerPolicy.defaultMode` is `null` | Add a mode word such as `deep research` or `deep review`. A mode hint works too. |
| The loop did not start | The mode was dispatched outside its command, so no workflow owned the state | Run `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. The command YAML owns state, dispatch and convergence. |
| The drift check reports `registry packet absent from hub index` | This README no longer names a packet folder that `mode-registry.json` registers | Add the folder name with a trailing slash to the README |

---

## 6. VERIFICATION

The parent-skill check validates the registry shape, the tool-surface union, the hub-router consistency and the ROUTER contract in one pass.

```bash
node .skilled/commands/doctor/scripts/parent-skill-check.cjs .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop
```

Expected output: `PASS:` lines for the checked invariants, with `INFO:` lines where the hub declares no surface packets. The summary reads `all hard invariants passed, 0 warnings`. Exit code 0.

```bash
node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/check-documentation-drift.cjs
```

Expected output: a JSON report whose `errors` array is empty. Exit code 0. The check confirms that every registered packet appears in this README with a trailing slash and that every benchmark report folder is indexed. It also fails on a broken relative link in the hub, packet, runtime and report docs.

```bash
python3 .skilled/skills/sk-doc/scripts/validate_document.py .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md --type readme
```

Expected output: `✅ VALID` and `Total issues: 0`.

---
title: "DRV-080 -- Frontier open veto holds a review stop, hard stops included"
description: "This scenario validates that under the expansive mode an open or active review frontier node turns a stop candidate into CONTINUE, hard stop candidates included, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first, for `DRV-080`."

---

# DRV-080 -- Frontier open veto holds a review stop, hard stops included

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-080`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the `frontierOpen` veto in the review stop decision for `DRV-080`. The objective is to confirm that a review stop waits while frontier nodes stay open, that the veto covers the clean stop that counts as a hard stop, and that every hard limit keeps its precedence.

### Why This Matters

A review that has checked every configured dimension looks finished. The veto keeps the review going while a sub-target is still open. It must never outrank a hard limit, or a review could loop past its budget. It must also cover the clean stop, or the veto would hold back only the soft stops.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the commands below and confirm the review veto table and the guard line that makes review candidates vetoable.

- Objective: Confirm that an open node vetoes a review stop candidate only under the expansive mode, that the veto covers `all_dimensions_clean`, which is a hard stop, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy keep their own decisions.
- Real user request: The review said it was clean, but it kept going. Why, and do the hard limits still stop it on time?
- Prompt: `Check that under expansive depth mode a review stop candidate continues while frontier nodes are open, including the clean stop, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the review veto table and the composite case by file stem, read the guard line that marks review candidates vetoable, then read the veto branch that sits after the floor check.
- Expected signals: All eight table rows pass. The row for open nodes returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen` for the clean stop. The row for an empty frontier returns `STOP` with `all_dimensions_clean`. The cap, floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and max-iterations rows keep their own decisions. The composite case returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen` while nodes are open and `STOP` with `composite_converged` once they drain. The guard line makes every review candidate vetoable.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say a review waits for its open sub-targets, clean stop included, and the hard limits still stop the run on time.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the table, the composite case and the guard line all pass or print as described; FAIL if an open node lets a review stop through, or a hard limit loses to the veto.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that under expansive depth mode a review stop candidate continues while frontier nodes are open, including the clean stop, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run stop-decision.vitest -t "expansive review candidate|vetoes a composite review stop while the frontier is open" --reporter=verbose`
2. `rg -n -F "loopType === 'review' || !candidate.hardStop" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  9 passed` (the rest of the file skipped). The eight table rows are named `expansive review candidate: <row label>`, and the composite case is `vetoes a composite review stop while the frontier is open`. The row `open nodes veto the clean stop` returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen`, and the row `an empty frontier lets the clean stop through` returns `STOP` with `all_dimensions_clean`. Command 2 prints one line, the guard that sets `vetoable` to true for every review loop and for any research candidate that is not a hard stop.

The table covers the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy. It does not cover consecutive failures or pause, so this scenario makes no claim about those two.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary line from command 1, with its exit status.
- The line from command 2, read from the current `convergence.cjs`.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Command 1 exits 0 with its nine cases passing, and command 2 prints the guard line.
- **Fail**: An open node lets a review stop through, the clean stop escapes the veto, a hard limit loses to the veto, or command 2 prints nothing.

### Failure Triage

1. An open node lets a review stop through: check that the veto reads the expansive flag and the count of open or active nodes from the review facts.
2. The clean stop escapes the veto: check the `vetoable` guard that command 2 prints. A review loop must stay vetoable whatever the candidate's `hardStop` value.
3. A hard limit loses to the veto: check the order of the veto and the cap, floor and stuck checks in the decision, since the veto must come after the floor.

---

### Playbook Sources

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `manual-testing-playbook.md` | Root directory page and scenario summary |

### Implementation And Test Anchors

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | The `frontierOpen` veto after the minimum-iteration floor, and the guard that makes review candidates vetoable |
| `stop-decision.vitest` | The review veto table, the composite case and the empty-frontier case |

title: "DR-072 -- Frontier open veto holds a soft stop"

version: 1.16.2.0
