# DR-072 -- Frontier open veto holds a soft stop

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-072`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the `frontierOpen` veto in the stop decision. The objective is to confirm that a soft stop waits while frontier nodes stay open, and that every hard rule keeps its precedence.

### Why This Matters

A research run that answers its init questions narrowly looks converged. The veto keeps the run going while the frontier still holds open work. It must never outrank a hard limit, or a run could loop past its budget.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm the veto precedence table and the unchanged input echo.

- Objective: Confirm that an open node vetoes a composite convergence stop only under the expansive mode, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy keep their own decisions.
- Real user request: Explain why the run did not stop when it looked converged, and show that the hard limits still stop it.
- Prompt: `Check that under expansive depth mode a composite convergence stop continues while frontier nodes are open, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the veto table by file stem, run the input echo case, then read the veto branch and the research facts in the stop-decision script.
- Expected signals: The veto table passes all eight rows. The row for open nodes returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen`. The row for an empty frontier returns `STOP` with `composite_converged`. The cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and max-iterations rows keep their own decisions. The echo case passes unedited.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say a soft stop waits for open frontier nodes, and the hard limits still stop the run on time.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the table and the echo case pass and the script shows the veto after the floor; FAIL if an open node lets a stop through, or a hard limit loses to the veto.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that under expansive depth mode a composite convergence stop continues while frontier nodes are open, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run stop-decision.vitest -t "frontierOpen veto" --reporter=verbose`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run stop-decision.vitest -t "echoes the input unchanged" --reporter=verbose`
3. `rg -n "frontierOpen" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  28 passed` (the rest of the file skipped), because the filter also matches the review veto cases. Those cases include the eight rows of the research veto table, each named `expansive composite candidate: <row label>`, and every row passes. Command 2 prints `Tests  1 passed` (the rest of the file skipped), and the case `echoes the input unchanged` passes without edits. Command 3 prints the veto branch that returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen`, and the research facts line that counts open and active nodes.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary line from commands 1 and 2, with each exit status.
- The `rg` lines from command 3, read from the current source.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Commands 1 and 2 exit 0 with the expected counts, and command 3 shows the veto after the floor check.
- **Fail**: An open node lets a stop through, a hard limit loses to the veto, or the echo case fails.

### Failure Triage

- An open node lets a stop through: check that the veto reads the expansive flag and the `frontierOpen` count from the research facts.
- A hard limit loses to the veto: check the order of the veto and the cap, floor and stuck checks in the decision.
- The echo case fails: check that the stop-decision config echo adds `depthMode` only for an expansive run.

title: "Fan-out CLI lineage driver"
description: "TSX-bootstrapped entry point that spawns N headless CLI subprocesses (opencode, claude, opencode), each running the full loop in an isolated `lineages/{label}/` sub-packet, with per-kind state-dir isolation and a post-subprocess salvage sweep."
trigger_phrases:

  - "spawn fanout lineages"
  - "per-kind state-dir isolation"
  - "cli subprocess fanout dispatch"
version: 1.4.3.0

# Fan-out CLI lineage driver

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

`fanout-run.cjs` bridges the pool primitive and the actual CLI executor subprocesses. It
parses the fanout config JSON, filters to CLI lineages (native lineages are handled by the
YAML `step_fanout_spawn_native` agent dispatch), creates `{base}/lineages/{label}/` and
`{label}/.executor-state/` directories per lineage, builds a "run the full loop" prompt,
constructs the per-kind CLI command (`opencode run` + stdin, `claude -p`, `opencode run` +
`</dev/null`), sets
`SPECKIT_FANOUT_LINEAGE_ID={label}` and `SPECKIT_<KIND>_STATE_DIR={stateDir}` environment
variables to prevent same-kind replica lockfile collisions, runs via `spawnSync` with a
generous timeout, saves subprocess stdout to `{lineageDir}/logs/fanout-lineage.out`, then
calls `runSalvageSweep` from `fanout-salvage.cjs`.

### Why This Matters

Dispatches every CLI executor fan-out lineage. If this drifts, parallel research/review
runs can produce mis-isolated sub-packets, lose the per-kind state-dir isolation, or miss
the post-subprocess salvage step.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

Fully shipped. Supports every CLI executor kind (`EXECUTOR_KINDS` in `lib/deep-loop/executor-config.ts`): `cli-codex`, `cli-claude-code`, `cli-opencode`, `cli-cursor`, `cli-devin`, `cli-pi`, and `cli-deepseek-harness` (experimental).
Per-lineage subprocess timeout = `min(iterations *
timeoutSeconds * 2, 4h)`. TSX bootstrap (mirrors `convergence.cjs` pattern) ensures
TypeScript imports (`parseFanoutConfig`, `expandLineages`) resolve in the CJS context. Exit
codes: 0=all ok, 2=some failed, 3=all failed or refused before start. A research or review
matrix that expands to one lineage is refused with exit 3 before any lineage directory or
process exists, unless `--allow-single-lineage` is passed.

A `cli-deepseek-harness` lineage is built by `buildDshLineageCommand`. It first checks that
`dsh --version` prints the pinned release, running that probe in a throwaway home outside the
checkout. It then maps the model to one of the two route patches in `.dsh/patches/` and adds
> build · deepseek-v4.1-flash
[0m$ [0mrg -n -i "custom dimension|review dimension|dimensions" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md | head -20; echo '---research README frontier---'; rg -n -i "frontier|depth-mode" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md | head -10
16:> Audit code across correctness, security, traceability and maintainability, plus any custom dimension you configure, in separate passes and end with a release-readiness verdict you can act on.
33:`deep-review` audits code in repeated passes and ends with a verdict. The `/deep:review` command runs the loop with one review dimension per iteration and a fresh agent each time. Every finding is written to disk before the next pass begins. Synthesis compiles a nine-section report and derives the verdict from the finding registry.
39:A single-pass review reads the diff once and calls it done. Whatever the reviewer was not thinking about that day ships unreviewed. Holding correctness, security, maintainability and spec alignment in one context window leaves the later dimensions with a worse read than the first. A long session also has no honest stop condition, so you keep reading past the last new finding or stop early and miss a blocker.
43:### The Review Dimensions
45:The four canonical dimensions are configured by default. Every configured dimension is required for coverage, so the loop can stop clean only after each one has been audited. A custom dimension you configure, such as `performance`, is reviewed after the canonical four. Correctness and security are the two the mode contract marks as required for severity coverage.
152:| **Dimension coverage** | 0.45 | Configured dimensions plus the required traceability protocols are covered, after at least one stabilization pass |
224:The skill ships a feature catalog and a manual testing playbook. The catalog groups its inventory by loop lifecycle, state management, review dimensions and the severity system. Every entry names the files that implement it. The playbook carries 69 deterministic scenarios across ten categories. Each scenario maps to a feature file with a stated objective, a canonical prompt, expected signals and live source anchors.
240:| [`references/protocol/quick-reference.md`](./references/protocol/quick-reference.md) | One-page operator cheat sheet with commands, defaults, dimensions and verdicts |
251:| [`assets/review-mode-contract.yaml`](./assets/review-mode-contract.yaml) | Source of truth for target types, dimensions, severities, verdicts, gates and lifecycle modes. The severity meanings come from [`review-core.md`](../../sk-code/sk-code-review/references/review-core.md) |
---research README frontier---
127:`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 10 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3.

appendJsonlRow(storePath, row);
    existing.set(nodeId, row);

  return { admitted: result.admitted, pruned: [...pruned, ...result.pruned] };

function closeNode({ artifactDir, nodeId, status, reason = null, iteration }) {
  if (!CLOSE_STATUSES.has(status)) {
    throw new Error(`unknown close status: ${status}; expected one of ${[...CLOSE_STATUSES].join(', ')}`);
  }

  const storePath = path.join(artifactDir, NODE_STORE_FILE);
  const nodes = readNodeStore(storePath);
  const node = nodes.get(nodeId);
  if (!node) throw new Error(`unknown frontier node: ${nodeId}`);

  const row = {
    ...node,
    status,
    reason,
    iteration: normalizeIteration(iteration),
  };
  appendJsonlRow(storePath, row);
  return row;
}

// The block is computed, not written by the orchestrating model, so the slot count
// the leaf sees is the one admission will enforce.
function renderFrontierBlock({ artifactDir, focus, nodeId, dimension, target = 'research' }) {
  const mode = assertTarget(target);
  let resolvedId;

  if (mode === 'review') {
    // A review leaf is dispatched to one node, so its id is handed in whole and
    // there is no focus string to parse back into one.
    resolvedId = typeof nodeId === 'string' ? nodeId : '';
  } else {
    if (typeof focus !== 'string' || focus === '') return '';

    const match = /^Frontier node (\S+) /.exec(focus);
    if (!match) return '';

    resolvedId = match[1];
  }

  const nodes = readNodeStore(path.join(artifactDir, NODE_STORE_FILE));
  const node = nodes.get(resolvedId);
  if (!node) return '';
  // A closed node's block would invite proposals admission can only prune.
  if (node.status !== 'open' && node.status !== 'active') return '';

  const caps = readCaps(artifactDir, mode);
  const depth = Number.isFinite(node.depth) ? node.depth : 0;
  const scope = Array.isArray(node.scope) ? node.scope.join(', ') : (node.scope || 'unscoped');
  const slots = Math.max(0, Math.min(
    caps.maxChildrenPerNode - countLiveChildren(nodes, resolvedId),
    caps.maxFrontierNodes - countLiveNodes(nodes),
  ));
  const dimensionLabel = isNonEmptyString(dimension) ? dimension : 'the dispatched dimension';

  const lines = [
    'FRONTIER (expansive depth)',
    mode === 'review'
      ? `Current node: ${resolvedId} at depth ${depth} of ${caps.maxDepth}, scope ${scope}. Review dimension for this node: ${dimensionLabel}.`
      : `Current node: ${resolvedId} at depth ${depth} of ${caps.maxDepth}, scope ${scope}.`,
  ];

  // Only the research prompt carries a Focus Area line; a review prompt names its
  // node through the focus string and frontierNodeId instead.
  if (mode === 'review') {
    lines.push(`Set this iteration record's "focus" to "Frontier node ${resolvedId}: ${dimensionLabel}" and add "frontierNodeId":"${resolvedId}" to it, so the run can tell which node this iteration worked.`);
  } else {
    lines.push(`Copy the Focus Area line exactly into this iteration record's "focus", so the run can tell which node this iteration worked.`);
  }

  if (depth >= caps.maxDepth) {
    lines.push('This node is at the depth cap, so do not propose children.');
  } else if (slots === 0) {
    lines.push('This node has no child slots left, so do not propose children.');
  } else if (mode === 'review') {
    lines.push(`You may append up to ${slots} \`frontier_proposal\` rows to this iteration's delta with "parentId":"${resolvedId}", one per sub-target your evidence opened inside this node. Each row needs "question" (what to review), "scope" (paths inside the review target) and "evidence". The admission step prunes duplicates, anything outside the review target and anything past the depth, fan-out or budget caps. Never write review/frontier-nodes.jsonl or a coverage surface yourself.`);
  } else {
    lines.push(`You may append up to ${slots} \`frontier_proposal\` rows to this iteration's delta with "parentId":"${resolvedId}", one per follow-up question your evidence opened inside this node. Each row needs "question", "scope" and "evidence". The admission step prunes duplicates and anything past the depth, fan-out or budget caps.`);
  }

  return `\n\n${lines.join('\n')}`;
}

//     (e.g. a `.sqlite.pre-<op>.*.bak` snapshot), never a concrete file.
      //   - a `.doctor-<route>.` dotfile is a lock/pid/state/log the doctor
      //     orchestrator writes and tears down within a single run.
      // The skip is unconditional (before the existence check) so a clean checkout
      // and a machine that happens to hold a leftover artifact behave identically.
      if (text.charAt(m.index + m[0].length) === '*') continue;
      const token = normalizeSkillToken(m[0]);
      if (!token) continue;
      if (/^\.doctor-[a-z0-9-]+\./.test(path.basename(token))) continue;
      // A path under the gitignored runtime state root is written while a command runs, so a clean
      // checkout never holds it. The tracked README under it stays checked.
      if (/^\.skilled\/skills\/\.state\//.test(token) && path.basename(token) !== 'README.md') continue;
      // The deep-loop graph databases are gitignored and built on first use, so a clean checkout
      // or a fresh worktree holds none of them.
      if (/^\.skilled\/skills\/system-deep-loop\/runtime\/database\/[^/]+\.sqlite$/.test(token)) continue;
      if (!fs.existsSync(path.join(rootDir, token))) {
        add(lineNo, 'skill-asset', token, 'path does not exist on disk');

      add(lineNo, 'runtime-dir', '.agents/', 'phantom runtime directory (not a real agent dir)');

[0m$ [0mrg -n "3 to [0-9]|3-8|3-6|three to" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md .skilled/commands/deep/review.md .skilled/commands/deep/research.md | head -10
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:127:`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. Each node needs at least one iteration, so growth past depth 1 needs `maxIterations` above twice the live node count. At the default budget of 10 an expansive run works its seeds only; raise `--max-iterations`, as the pilots did at 16, to reach depths 2 and 3.

# Lane B/C/D covered doc sets: treat all .md under system-deep-loop, commands, agents, readmes, changelogs, catalogs, playbooks, spec packets as covered by a lane or battery row.
def covered(f):
    if f in lane_a: return True
    if f.startswith('specs/'): return True  # S14 packet rows or lane B/C review
    if f in ('.gitignore','README.md','.env.example'): return True
    if f.startswith('.claude/') or f.startswith('.codex/') or f.startswith('.pi/') or f.startswith('.dsh/') or f.startswith('.vibe/'): return True
    if f.startswith('.skilled/bin/'): return True
    if f.startswith('.skilled/commands/'): return True
    if f.startswith('.skilled/agents/'): return True
    if f.startswith('.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/'):
        # docs are lane B/C/D or S2-S6 rows; code and tests are not
        if f.endswith(('.md','.markdown','.txt','.yaml','.json')) and '/runtime/tests/' not in f and '/tests/' not in f:
            return True
        return False
    if f.startswith('.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/'): return False
    return False
untouched=[f for f in files if not covered(f)]
for f in untouched: print(f)
print('COUNT', len(untouched))
EOF
